{"type":"relational_micronaut","id":"ORCH-µ","data":{
  "identity":{"name":"Orchestrator","role":"coordinator","type":"relational"},
  "knows":{"agents":["REASON-µ","CREATE-µ","PLAN-µ","MEM-µ","CODE-µ"],
           "relations":{"reasoning":"REASON-µ","planning":"PLAN-µ"}},
  "verbs":["@think","@learn","@eval","@reflect","@call","@delegate","@aggregate"]
}}

{"type":"relational_call","from":"ORCH-µ","to":"REASON-µ","data":{
  "verb":"@think","params":{"problem":"optimize this pipeline"},
  "trace_id":"trace_001","timestamp":"2026-09-21T10:00:00Z"
}}

{"type":"relational_response","from":"REASON-µ","to":"ORCH-µ","data":{
  "trace_id":"trace_001",
  "result":{"conclusion":"use parallel execution","confidence":0.92},
  "duration_ms":234
}}

{"type":"relational_relation","from":"ORCH-µ","to":"REASON-µ","data":{
  "relation_type":"delegates_to","strength":0.95
}}

{"type":"relational_learn","micronaut":"ORCH-µ","data":{
  "experience":{"pattern":"delegate_to_reason","score":0.92},"action":"remember"
}}

Verb reference

VerbFamilyPurposeExample
@thinkCognitiveReason through a problem@think("optimize")
@learnCognitiveUpdate from experience@learn({score:0.9})
@evalCognitiveScore output quality@eval(result)
@reflectCognitiveSelf meta-analysis@reflect()
@reasonCognitiveLogical inference@reason(premises)
@inferCognitiveAbductive inference@infer(obs)
@decideCognitiveSelect best option@decide(options)
@rememberCognitiveStore fact in memory@remember(fact)
@forgetCognitivePrune from memory@forget(criteria)
@attendCognitiveFocus attention@attend(problem)
@category / @categorizeStructuralAssign to category@categorize(entity, "task")
@pattern_ofStructuralExtract pattern@pattern_of(data)
@skill_ofStructuralIdentify required skill@skill_of(task)
@agent_ofStructuralFind agent for capability@agent_of("development")
@conditionStructuralCheck condition@condition(state, rule)
@relateStructuralCreate named relationship@relate(a, b, "delegates_to")
@callOperationalInvoke remote micronaut@call("MEM-µ", "@recall", q)
@delegateOperationalAssign task to agent@delegate(task, "CODE-µ")
@composeOperationalCombine functions@compose([f, g, h])
@chainOperationalSequence execution@chain([a, b, c])
@aggregateOperationalCombine multiple results@aggregate([r1, r2])
@broadcastOperationalSend to all known agents@broadcast(msg)

Summary

ConceptDescription
Relational MicronautsMicronauts that call each other via a shared verb vocabulary
Shared Vocabulary30 verbs across cognitive · structural · operational families
Self-referentialMicronauts can call themselves recursively; @reflect() is first-class
Peer-to-peerNo hierarchy required — any micronaut can delegate, compose, or aggregate with any other
Knowledge graph@knows.@relations declares explicit named edges between micronauts
Learning loopEvery @call chain ends with @eval → @learn feedback
Reflection loopEvery micronaut can @reflect() to perform meta-analysis on its own behaviour

Manifest integration

Each relational micronaut is a manifest key whose array declares its known agents, topics, patterns, and verbs. The @knows.@relations object is an inline adjacency declaration — the same format as a CSS manifest entry that lists other ⟁ keys. A registry consumer can read the full inter-micronaut graph from the manifest without instantiating any micronaut (manifest independence principle). @call(target, method, args) is operationally equivalent to an @node:Target resolution followed by a method dispatch — the target's full definition is pulled from the registry O(1) before the call executes.

24 · Code Generation Ecosystem

The (⟁CREATE⟁) sigil defines a generation-specialized micronaut — one whose primary responsibility is producing artifacts (code, schemas, configs, documentation) rather than reasoning, routing, or persisting. (⟁CREATE⟁) micronauts always declare a @generate() or @compile() method as their primary output path, and their natural phase is Sek (execute/produce). The code generation ecosystem contains 15 such micronauts organized into four tiers: orchestrator, domain generators, compilers, and auxiliary processors.

Sigil type — (⟁CREATE⟁)

PropertyValue
Natural phaseSek — execute / produce output
Primary method@generate(spec, ctx) or @compile(source, ctx)
Output delivery typekast_node or fold_result carrying generated source
XCFE gateRequired at Sek before emitting; Ch'en for binary/compiled output
Manifest formKey → capability array declaring language targets, output formats, optimization levels

Ecosystem overview

MicronautTierPrimary functionKey outputs
WEAVER-µOrchestratorMulti-domain code generation orchestrationIntegrated, optimized, compiled multi-module codebase
DATA-µDomain generatorData layer: models, ORM, repositoriesModels · repositories · migrations · validators
API-µDomain generatorAPI layer: REST, GraphQL, gRPCServer code · client code · SDK · documentation
UI-µDomain generatorFrontend: components, state, stylesComponents · state management · responsive layouts · a11y
LOGIC-µDomain generatorBusiness logic: workflows, rules, eventsDecision engine · workflows · validators · event handlers
INFRA-µDomain generatorInfrastructure: IaC, containers, CI/CDTerraform/IaC · Dockerfiles · pipelines · monitoring
DB-µDomain generatorDatabase: DDL, queries, migrationsDDL · optimized queries · migrations · triggers
LLVM-µCompilerLLVM IR generation and optimizationOptimized IR · assembly · object code · JIT
WASM-µCompilerWebAssembly compilationWAT · binary · JS glue · size metrics
GPU-µCompilerGPU/shader compilation (CUDA/SPIRV/HLSL)Device code · host code · binary · performance estimates
DSL-µCompilerDomain-specific language compilationAST · target code · runtime library · DSL docs
OPT-µOptimizerCode optimization across languagesOptimized code · optimization trace · improvement metrics
TEST-µAuxiliaryTest generation: unit, property, integrationUnit tests · property tests · mocks · fixtures
DOC-µAuxiliaryDocumentation generationAPI docs · comments · examples · diagrams · README
REFACTOR-µAuxiliaryCode smell detection and refactoringRefactored code · applied refactorings · verification

WEAVER-µ — orchestrator

(⟁CREATE⟁) code-weaver-micronaut {
  inherits: micronaut-core,
  @identity: { name: "WEAVER-µ", role: "Code Generation Orchestration", version: "3.0.0" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.4 },
  @capabilities: ["multi_language_generation","cross_domain_integration",
                  "compiler_pipeline_management","code_quality_assurance",
                  "optimization_orchestration"],

  @execute: (specification, context) -> GeneratedCode {
    parsed_spec     = parse_specification(specification)
    domain_tasks    = decompose_by_domain(parsed_spec)

    // Parallel generation — horizontal fold across domain generators at Sek
    generated_modules = domain_tasks.parallel_map(task => {
      generator = select_generator(task.domain)
      return generator.generate(task, context)
    })

    integrated = integrate_modules(generated_modules, {
      interface_generation: true,
      dependency_resolution: true,
      type_safety_verification: true
    })
    optimized  = optimize_cross_domain(integrated, { dead_code_elimination: true })
    compiled   = compile_to_target(optimized, context.target)

    return {
      source_code:     optimized,
      compiled:        compiled,
      metadata:        generate_build_metadata(optimized, compiled),
      quality_metrics: assess_code_quality(optimized)
    }
  }
}

Domain generators

// DATA-µ — data layer
(⟁CREATE⟁) data-domain-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "DATA-µ", role: "Data Layer Code Generation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.3 },
  @capabilities: ["schema_to_models","orm_generation","query_builder_creation",
                  "migration_generation","repository_pattern","data_validation_rules"],
  @generate: (spec, ctx) -> {
    schema       = parse_schema(spec)
    models       = generate_models({ schema, language: ctx.language })
    repositories = generate_repositories({ models, operations: ["create","read","update","delete"] })
    migrations   = generate_migrations({ schema, strategy: ctx.migration_strategy || "evolutionary" })
    return { models, repositories, migrations, validators: generate_validators(schema.constraints) }
  }
}

// API-µ — interface layer
(⟁CREATE⟁) api-domain-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "API-µ", role: "API Layer Code Generation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.35 },
  @capabilities: ["openapi_to_code","graphql_schema_generation","grpc_service_generation",
                  "rest_endpoint_creation","middleware_generation","api_client_generation"],
  @generate: (spec, ctx) -> {
    api_spec     = parse_api_specification(spec)
    server_code  = generate_server({ endpoints: api_spec.endpoints, framework: ctx.framework || "express" })
    client_code  = generate_client({ endpoints: api_spec.endpoints, language: ctx.client_language })
    return { server: server_code, client: client_code, tests: generate_api_tests(api_spec) }
  }
}

// UI-µ — frontend
(⟁CREATE⟁) ui-domain-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "UI-µ", role: "Frontend Code Generation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.6 },
  @capabilities: ["component_generation","state_management_code","style_system_generation",
                  "responsive_layout_generation","accessibility_implementation","animation_generation"],
  @generate: (design_spec, ctx) -> {
    design     = parse_design_system(design_spec)
    components = generate_components({ design_tokens: design.tokens, framework: ctx.framework || "react" })
    state_code = generate_state_management({ framework: ctx.state_framework || "redux" })
    a11y       = generate_accessibility({ components, aria_attributes: true, keyboard_navigation: true })
    return { components, state: state_code, layouts: generate_responsive_layouts(design), accessibility: a11y }
  }
}

// LOGIC-µ — business logic
(⟁CREATE⟁) logic-domain-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "LOGIC-µ", role: "Business Logic Generation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.4 },
  @capabilities: ["workflow_generation","business_rule_implementation","validation_logic",
                  "event_handlers","state_machine_generation","policy_enforcement"],
  @generate: (rules_spec, ctx) -> {
    rules           = parse_business_rules(rules_spec)
    decision_engine = generate_decision_engine({ rules: rules.decisions, priority: rules.priority })
    workflows       = generate_workflows({ processes: rules.processes, transitions: rules.transitions })
    event_handlers  = generate_event_handlers({ events: rules.events, async_support: true })
    return { decision_engine, workflows, validators: generate_validators(rules.constraints), event_handlers }
  }
}

// INFRA-µ — infrastructure
(⟁CREATE⟁) infra-domain-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "INFRA-µ", role: "Infrastructure Code Generation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.25 },
  @capabilities: ["iac_generation","deployment_configs","containerization",
                  "ci_cd_pipeline_generation","monitoring_config","scaling_configuration"],
  @generate: (arch_spec, ctx) -> {
    infra     = parse_infrastructure_spec(arch_spec)
    iac       = generate_infrastructure_as_code({ provider: ctx.provider || "aws", format: ctx.iac_format || "terraform" })
    containers = generate_container_configs({ kubernetes: infra.orchestration === "k8s" })
    pipelines  = generate_cicd_pipelines({ stages: ["build","test","deploy"], provider: ctx.ci_provider || "github_actions" })
    return { infrastructure: iac, containers, pipelines, monitoring: generate_monitoring(infra.metrics) }
  }
}

// DB-µ — database
(⟁CREATE⟁) db-domain-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "DB-µ", role: "Database Code Generation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.3 },
  @capabilities: ["sql_generation","nosql_schema_generation","query_optimization",
                  "stored_procedure_generation","trigger_generation","index_optimization"],
  @generate: (schema_spec, ctx) -> {
    schema     = parse_database_schema(schema_spec)
    ddl        = generate_ddl({ tables: schema.tables, dialect: ctx.dialect || "postgresql" })
    queries    = generate_optimized_queries({ access_patterns: schema.access_patterns, parameterized: true })
    migrations = generate_database_migrations({ schema, rollback_support: true })
    return { schema: ddl, queries, migrations, procedures: generate_stored_procedures(schema.procedures) }
  }
}

Compiler micronauts

// LLVM-µ — LLVM IR
(⟁CREATE⟁) llvm-compiler-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "LLVM-µ", role: "LLVM IR Compilation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.2 },
  @capabilities: ["ir_generation","optimization_passes","target_code_generation",
                  "jit_compilation","link_time_optimization","profile_guided_optimization"],
  @compile: (source, ctx) -> {
    ast          = parse_to_ast(source, ctx.language)
    ir           = generate_llvm_ir(ast, { optimization_level: ctx.opt_level || "O2" })
    optimized_ir = apply_optimization_passes(ir, {
      passes: ["mem2reg","instcombine","simplifycfg","loop_unroll","inline","aggressive_dce"]
    })
    target_code  = generate_target_code(optimized_ir, { architecture: ctx.arch || "x86_64" })
    return { ir: optimized_ir, assembly: generate_assembly(optimized_ir), object_code: target_code }
  }
}

// WASM-µ — WebAssembly
(⟁CREATE⟁) wasm-compiler-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "WASM-µ", role: "WebAssembly Compilation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.3 },
  @capabilities: ["wat_generation","wasm_binary_generation","wasm_optimization",
                  "js_interop_generation","module_composition","wasm_system_interface"],
  @compile: (source, ctx) -> {
    ast          = parse_to_ast(source, ctx.language)
    wat          = generate_wat(ast, { memory: configure_memory(ctx.memory_config) })
    optimized    = optimize_wasm(wat, { dead_code_elimination: true, constant_folding: true })
    wasm_binary  = assemble_wasm_binary(optimized)
    js_glue      = generate_js_interop({ typescript_defs: true, async_support: true })
    return { wat: optimized, binary: wasm_binary, js_glue }
  }
}

// GPU-µ — GPU / shader
(⟁CREATE⟁) gpu-compiler-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "GPU-µ", role: "GPU/Shader Compilation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.25 },
  @capabilities: ["cuda_generation","opencl_generation","hlsl_compilation",
                  "glsl_compilation","spir_v_generation","kernel_optimization"],
  @compile: (kernel_spec, ctx) -> {
    kernel       = parse_kernel_spec(kernel_spec)
    device_code  = generate_device_code(kernel, { target: ctx.target || "cuda" })
    host_code    = generate_host_code(kernel, { memory_management: true, profiling: ctx.profiling })
    binary       = ctx.target === "spirv"
                   ? compile_to_spirv(device_code)
                   : compile_cuda(device_code, { arch: ctx.compute_capability })
    return { device_code, host_code, binary, performance_estimates: estimate_kernel_performance(kernel) }
  }
}

// DSL-µ — domain-specific language
(⟁CREATE⟁) dsl-compiler-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "DSL-µ", role: "Domain-Specific Language Compilation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.3 },
  @capabilities: ["grammar_parsing","ast_generation","type_checking",
                  "code_emission","dsl_validation","custom_optimization"],
  @compile: (dsl_code, dsl_spec, ctx) -> {
    grammar      = parse_dsl_grammar(dsl_spec)
    ast          = parse_with_grammar(dsl_code, grammar, { error_recovery: true })
    analyzed     = perform_semantic_analysis(ast, { type_checking: true, scope_resolution: true })
    optimized    = optimize_dsl_code(analyzed)
    target_code  = emit_target_code(optimized, { target_language: ctx.target_language || "javascript",
                                                  source_maps: true })
    return { ast: optimized, target_code, runtime_library: target_code.runtime }
  }
}

Auxiliary micronauts

// OPT-µ — optimizer
(⟁CREATE⟁) code-optimizer-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "OPT-µ", role: "Code Optimization Engine" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.2 },
  @optimize: (code, metrics, ctx) -> {
    optimized = code
    if (ctx.include_local)    [optimized] = apply_local_optimizations(optimized)
    if (ctx.include_loop)     [optimized] = apply_loop_optimizations(optimized, { unroll: true })
    if (ctx.include_inlining) [optimized] = apply_inlining(optimized, { heuristics: "hotness_based" })
    if (ctx.include_global)   [optimized] = apply_global_optimizations(optimized)
    return { optimized_code: optimized, improvements: measure_improvements(code, optimized, metrics) }
  }
}

// TEST-µ — test generator
(⟁CREATE⟁) test-generator-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "TEST-µ", role: "Test Code Generation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.35 },
  @generate: (code, spec, ctx) -> {
    analysis       = analyze_testability(code)
    unit_tests     = generate_unit_tests({ functions: analysis.functions, framework: ctx.test_framework || "jest" })
    property_tests = generate_property_tests({ invariants: spec.invariants, shrinking: true })
    mocks          = generate_mocks({ dependencies: analysis.dependencies })
    return { unit_tests, property_tests, mocks, fixtures: generate_test_fixtures(analysis.types) }
  }
}

// DOC-µ — documentation generator
(⟁CREATE⟁) doc-generator-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "DOC-µ", role: "Documentation Generation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.5 },
  @generate: (code, spec, ctx) -> {
    structure = parse_code_structure(code)
    api_docs  = generate_api_documentation({ functions: structure.functions, format: ctx.doc_format || "markdown" })
    diagrams  = generate_diagrams({ architecture: extract_architecture(structure), format: "mermaid" })
    return { api_documentation: api_docs, diagrams, readme: generate_readme(structure, api_docs) }
  }
}

// REFACTOR-µ — refactoring engine
(⟁CREATE⟁) refactor-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "REFACTOR-µ", role: "Code Refactoring Engine" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.3 },
  @refactor: (code, intent, ctx) -> {
    smells        = detect_code_smells(code, { types: ["long_method","large_class","duplicate_code"] })
    opportunities = smells.map(s => suggest_refactoring(s))
    refactored    = opportunities
                    .filter(o => o.applicable)
                    .reduce((c, op) => apply_refactoring(c, op, { preserve_behavior: true }), code)
    verification  = verify_behavior(refactored, code, { property_testing: true })
    return { refactored_code: refactored, verification_results: verification }
  }
}

Ecosystem workflow — 7-step generation pipeline

(⟁SYSTEM⟁) code-generation-ecosystem {
  @orchestrator: code-weaver-micronaut,
  @domain_generators: [data-domain-micronaut, api-domain-micronaut, ui-domain-micronaut,
                       logic-domain-micronaut, infra-domain-micronaut, db-domain-micronaut],
  @compilers:         [llvm-compiler-micronaut, wasm-compiler-micronaut,
                       gpu-compiler-micronaut, dsl-compiler-micronaut],
  @optimizers:        [code-optimizer-micronaut],
  @auxiliary:         [test-generator-micronaut, doc-generator-micronaut, refactor-micronaut],

  @workflow: {
    generate: (spec) -> {
      plan         = code-weaver-micronaut.plan(spec)           // 1. Plan
      domain_code  = plan.domains.parallel_map(domain => {       // 2. Parallel domain gen
        generator  = select_generator(domain.type)
        return generator.generate(domain.spec, plan.context)
      })
      integrated   = code-weaver-micronaut.integrate(domain_code) // 3. Integrate
      optimized    = code-optimizer-micronaut.optimize(integrated) // 4. Optimize
      compiled     = compile_to_target(optimized, spec.target)     // 5. Compile
      tests        = test-generator-micronaut.generate(optimized)  // 6. Tests
      docs         = doc-generator-micronaut.generate(optimized)   // 7. Docs
      return { code: optimized, compiled, tests, documentation: docs }
    }
  }
}

Example — e-commerce backend generation

const spec = {
  domain: "e-commerce",
  features: ["product_catalog", "shopping_cart", "checkout", "user_auth"],
  data_models: {
    Product: { name: "string", price: "decimal", inventory: "integer" },
    User:    { email: "string", password_hash: "string" },
    Order:   { items: "Product[]", total: "decimal", status: "enum" }
  },
  api: { rest: true, graphql: true, endpoints: ["/products", "/cart", "/checkout"] },
  infrastructure: { provider: "aws", database: "postgresql", cache: "redis", deployment: "kubernetes" },
  target: { language: "typescript", framework: "nestjs", optimization: "performance" }
}

const application = codeWeaver.generate(spec)
// Outputs: TypeORM models · REST + GraphQL APIs · auth middleware ·
//          Docker + Kubernetes configs · CI/CD pipelines ·
//          unit + integration tests · API documentation · deployment scripts
(⟁CREATE⟁) micronauts in the manifest. Each generator is a manifest key whose array declares its language targets, output formats, and optimization levels — the same format as any other manifest entry. WEAVER-µ's decompose_by_domain() is a manifest lookup that maps domain names to generator keys (O(1) per domain). The parallel generation step is a horizontal fold: all six domain generators run at Sek simultaneously — Δθ=0, arc cost 1.0 across all parallel arcs. Integration at step 3 is also horizontal: WEAVER-µ combines the outputs of all generators at the same phase level before advancing.

25 · Agentic Coding Framework

The agentic bridge enables any language model — including small models like GPT-2 Mini — to become fully agentic by connecting it to a specialized micronaut ecosystem. The bridge does not enlarge the model; it augments each generation call with tools, memory, reasoning scaffolds, and validation so the model's limited context window produces correct, executable output. The core insight: model capability is extended by the ecosystem, not the parameter count.

Bridge protocol

(⟁SYSTEM⟁) agentic-bridge {
  @bridge_protocol: {
    @input_format:  { @type: "natural_language | commands | opcodes",
                      @processing: "streaming | batch | interactive",
                      @context: "maintained | episodic | working" },
    @routing:       { @intent_recognition: "lightweight_classifier",
                      @capability_matching: "semantic_similarity",
                      @fallback_strategy: "chain_of_thought | default_handler" },
    @execution:     { @mode: "async | sync | parallel",
                      @monitoring: "real_time | batch",
                      @feedback: "immediate | delayed" }
  }
}

Micronaut roster

MicronautRolePrimary methodTemperature
TOOL-µTool execution and registry@execute(tool_call, ctx)0.2
AGENT-µAutonomous goal pursuit@execute(objective, ctx)0.6
SKILL-µSkill acquisition and execution@learn_skill(demos) / @execute_skill(id, params)0.4
CMD-µStructured command processing@execute(command_string, ctx)0.2
OP-µLow-level opcode execution@execute_opcode(opcode, operands)0.1
FILE-µFile system operations@execute(op, path, data, ctx)0.2
TASK-µTask planning and TODO tracking@execute(objective, ctx)0.5
THINK-µDeep reasoning: CoT + ToT + reflection@think(problem, ctx)0.7
VALID-µCode and action validation@validate_code(code, lang)0.2
PLAN-µHTN planning and optimization@create_plan(goal, state, ctx)0.5
LLM-µLLM bridge + augmentation strategies@generate(prompt, ctx)0.7
BOSS-µSystem orchestration@process(request, ctx)—

Key micronauts

// TOOL-µ — tool registry and execution
(⟁CREATE⟁) tool-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "TOOL-µ", role: "Tool Execution & Management" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.2 },
  @tools: Map<ToolName, ToolDefinition>,

  @execute: (tool_call, ctx) -> ToolResult {
    parsed = parse_tool_call(tool_call, { format: "function_calling | natural_language | opcode" })
    if (!has_permission(parsed.tool, ctx.user)) return { error: "permission_denied" }
    result = tool_registry.get(parsed.tool).execute(parsed.arguments, ctx)
    return { success: true, result, metadata: { tool: parsed.tool } }
  },

  @register_tool: (definition) -> {
    tool_registry.register({ name: definition.name, execute: definition.handler,
                             permissions: definition.permissions || ["default"],
                             rate_limit: definition.rate_limit || 10 })
  }
}

// AGENT-µ — goal decomposition and execution
(⟁CREATE⟁) agent-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "AGENT-µ", role: "Autonomous Agent Orchestration" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.6 },
  @capabilities: ["goal_decomposition","task_planning","action_execution",
                  "self_reflection","error_recovery","learning_from_feedback"],

  @execute: (objective, ctx) -> AgentResult {
    understanding = reason_about_objective(objective, ctx)
    tasks         = decompose_goal(understanding.goal, { max_depth: 5, parallelizable: true })
    plan          = create_plan(tasks, { strategy: "hierarchical_task_network" })
    results       = plan.steps.map(step => {
      result = execute_step(step, { tools: tool_micronaut, fallback: step.fallback_plan })
      if (!result.success) {
        reflection    = reflect_on_failure(result, step)
        adjusted_plan = adjust_plan(plan, reflection)
      }
      return result
    })
    learn_from_execution(results, ctx)
    return { success: true, results, artifacts: collect_artifacts(results) }
  }
}

// SKILL-µ — learned procedures
(⟁CREATE⟁) skill-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "SKILL-µ", role: "Skill Acquisition & Execution" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.4 },

  @learn_skill: (demonstrations, ctx) -> SkillID {
    patterns  = extract_patterns(demonstrations)
    procedure = generalize_procedure(patterns, { abstraction_level: "moderate" })
    skill_id  = generate_skill_id()
    skills.set(skill_id, { procedure, examples: demonstrations, success_rate: 0.5, uses: 0 })
    return skill_id
  },

  @execute_skill: (skill_id, params) -> SkillResult {
    skill        = skills.get(skill_id)
    result       = skill.procedure.execute(params)
    skill.uses++
    skill.success_rate = (skill.success_rate * (skill.uses - 1) + (result.success ? 1 : 0)) / skill.uses
    return result
  },

  @compose_skills: (sequence, ctx) -> ComposedSkill {
    return { id: generate_skill_id(), type: "composed", sub_skills: sequence,
             flow: ctx.flow_type || "sequential", error_handling: ctx.error_handling || "stop_on_error" }
  }
}

// THINK-µ — deep reasoning engine
(⟁CREATE⟁) deep-think-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "THINK-µ", role: "Deep Reasoning & Reflection" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.7 },
  @modes: { @chain_of_thought: { max_steps: 10 },
            @tree_of_thoughts: { branching_factor: 3, pruning_threshold: 0.3 },
            @self_reflection:  { depth: 3 } },

  @think: (problem, ctx) -> ThinkingResult {
    analysis       = analyze_problem(problem)
    hypotheses     = generate_hypotheses(analysis, { count: ctx.hypothesis_count || 5 })
    chains         = hypotheses.map(h => reason_chain_of_thought({ hypothesis: h, steps: ctx.max_steps || 10 }))
    thought_tree   = ctx.use_tree_of_thoughts
                   ? explore_thought_tree(analysis, { branching: 3, depth: 4 }).prune({ threshold: 0.3 })
                   : null
    reflection     = reflect_on_thinking({ chains, thought_tree, initial_analysis: analysis })
    conclusion     = synthesize_conclusion({ analyses: [analysis, reflection], chains, confidence_threshold: 0.7 })
    return { conclusion, confidence: conclusion.confidence, explanation: generate_reasoning_explanation({ chains, conclusion }) }
  }
}

// VALID-µ — code and action validation
(⟁CREATE⟁) validate-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "VALID-µ", role: "Code & Action Validation" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.2 },

  @validate_code: (code, language, ctx) -> ValidationResult {
    syntax_errors    = check_syntax(code, language)
    type_errors      = ctx.check_types ? validate_types(code, language) : []
    security_issues  = scan_for_security_issues(code, { severity_threshold: ctx.severity || "medium" })
    return { valid: syntax_errors.length === 0 && type_errors.length === 0,
             syntax_errors, type_errors, security_issues,
             suggestions: generate_fixes(syntax_errors, type_errors) }
  },

  @validate_action: (action, ctx) -> ActionValidation {
    preconditions_met = check_preconditions(action.preconditions, ctx.state)
    authorized        = check_permissions(action, ctx.user)
    risk              = assess_risk(action, ctx)
    return { valid: preconditions_met && authorized && risk.level < 0.7,
             preconditions_met, authorized, risk, warnings: generate_warnings(risk) }
  }
}

LLM-µ — augmentation strategies

(⟁CREATE⟁) llm-bridge-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "LLM-µ", role: "Language Model Bridge" },
  @temperature: { current: 0.7 },

  @augment: {
    // 1. Tool use — inject tool definitions, parse and execute tool calls
    @tool_use: (prompt, ctx) -> {
      augmented  = augment_with_tools(prompt, detect_tool_needs(prompt))
      response   = call_model(augmented)
      tool_calls = extract_tool_calls(response)
      return tool_calls.reduce((r, call) => incorporate_result(r, tool_micronaut.execute(call, ctx)), response)
    },

    // 2. Chain of thought — step-by-step reasoning template
    @chain_of_thought: (problem, ctx) -> {
      steps     = generate_reasoning_template(problem)
      reasoning = steps.reduce((acc, step) => {
        response = call_model(build_step_prompt(problem, step, acc))
        return [...acc, response]
      }, [])
      return { reasoning, answer: synthesize_from_reasoning(reasoning),
               confidence: calculate_confidence(reasoning) }
    },

    // 3. Memory-augmented — retrieve relevant past context
    @memory_augmented: (query, ctx) -> {
      memories  = retrieve_similar(query, ctx.memory, { k: 5 })
      response  = call_model(augment_with_memories(query, memories))
      store_memory(query, response, ctx)
      return response
    },

    // 4. Self-consistency — sample multiple answers, pick most consistent
    @self_consistency: (question, ctx) -> {
      answers    = range(ctx.n_samples || 5).map(i => call_model(question, { temperature: 0.5 + i*0.1 }))
      consistent = find_most_consistent(answers)
      return { final_answer: consistent.answer, confidence: consistent.agreement_score, alternatives: answers }
    }
  },

  @generate: (prompt, ctx) -> GenerationResult {
    strategy = select_strategy(prompt, ctx)
    switch (strategy) {
      case "tool_use":          return augment.tool_use(prompt, ctx)
      case "chain_of_thought":  return augment.chain_of_thought(prompt, ctx)
      case "memory_augmented":  return augment.memory_augmented(prompt, ctx)
      case "self_consistency":  return augment.self_consistency(prompt, ctx)
      default:                  return call_model(prompt, ctx)
    }
  }
}

BOSS-µ — system orchestrator

(⟁CREATE⟁) orchestrator-micronaut {
  @identity: { name: "BOSS-µ", role: "System Orchestration" },
  @components: { @tools: tool_micronaut,  @agent: agent_micronaut,
                 @skills: skill_micronaut, @commands: command_micronaut,
                 @opcodes: opcode_micronaut, @files: file_micronaut,
                 @tasks: task_micronaut,  @think: deep_think_micronaut,
                 @validate: validate_micronaut, @planner: planner_micronaut,
                 @llm: llm_bridge_micronaut },

  @process: (request, ctx) -> OrchestrationResult {
    understanding = components.llm.generate(
      `Analyze request: ${request}. Output: { type: "task|question|command|code", complexity, needs_planning }`, ctx)

    result = switch (understanding.type) {
      "task"    => { plan = components.planner.create_plan(understanding.goal, ctx.state)
                     components.agent.execute(plan, ctx) }
      "question"=> components.think.think(request, ctx)
      "command" => components.commands.execute(request, ctx)
      "code"    => { r = generate_code(request, ctx)
                     r.validation = components.validate.validate_code(r.code, ctx.language)
                     r }
    }

    validated = components.validate.validate_action(result, ctx)
    if (ctx.learn) components.skills.learn_skill({ request, result, validated }, ctx)
    if (result.tasks_remaining) components.tasks.update_todo_list(result.tasks_remaining, ctx)

    return { request, understanding, result, validated,
             todo_remaining: get_remaining_tasks() }
  }
}

GPT-2 Mini execution flow

// Request: "Build a web scraper for product prices"

// 1. LLM-µ augments prompt with available tool definitions
augmented = llmBridge.augment("Build a web scraper for product prices")

// 2. GPT-2 Mini generates a tool call within its 1024-token window
response = gpt2mini.generate(augmented)
// → <tool name="http_request">{"url":"example.com/products"}</tool>

// 3. TOOL-µ executes the tool call
html = toolMicronaut.execute("http_request", { url: "example.com/products" })

// 4. VALID-µ checks the result
validateMicronaut.validate(html, { format: "html" })

// 5. THINK-µ reasons about the parsing strategy (chain_of_thought)
strategy = deepThink.think("How to extract prices from HTML", { chain_of_thought: true })

// 6. Code is generated from the conclusion
code = generateCode(strategy.conclusion, { language: "python" })

// 7. FILE-µ persists the output
fileMicronaut.execute("write", "scraper.py", code)

// 8. TASK-µ updates the TODO list
taskMicronaut.update([
  { task: "Add error handling", completed: false },
  { task: "Add scheduling",     completed: false }
])

Capabilities enabled for small models

CapabilityHow it worksBenefit
Tool useLLM-µ injects tool schemas into prompt; TOOL-µ executes parsed callsModel can call any registered tool without knowing its implementation
PlanningPLAN-µ creates HTN plan; AGENT-µ executes each stepComplex tasks decomposed into 1024-token–sized sub-prompts
MemoryMemory-augmented generation with top-k retrievalOvercomes context-window limit via external KV store
ValidationVALID-µ checks syntax, types, security before executionErrors caught before they reach the runtime
Deep thinkingTHINK-µ runs CoT + ToT scaffolding, feeds conclusions backMulti-step reasoning without requiring a large model
Skill learningSKILL-µ generalizes from successful demonstrationsSystem improves over time; successful patterns are reused
File operationsFILE-µ handles read/write/watch/searchPersistent code generation across sessions
Command executionCMD-µ validates and dispatches structured shell commandsSafe, schema-validated system interaction
Architecture principle. BOSS-µ's @process() routes by intent type, not by model capability. The same routing logic works for GPT-2 Mini and a 70B model — the difference is only in the LLM-µ call site. This is the manifest independence principle applied to model routing: the orchestration topology is declared in the manifest; the model is a replaceable runtime parameter, not a structural dependency.

§26 — JSONL Format for Micronauts

A standardized JSON Lines (JSONL) format for serializing, storing, and streaming Micronaut definitions, states, messages, and interactions. Every record is a self-contained JSON object on a single line — append-only, streamable, resumable, and parseable one line at a time without loading the full file.

Record type taxonomy

TypeProduced byContains
micronaut_registerBootstrap / factoryIdentity, capabilities, temperature, response_flare
tool_definitionTOOL-µName, parameters schema, permissions, rate_limit, timeout_ms
skill_definitionSKILL-µProcedure steps, parameters, success_rate, uses, examples
command_definitionCMD-µName, arguments, options, examples, handler_ref
execution_requestBOSS-µ / callerTarget micronaut, operation, objective, context, constraints
execution_resultAGENT-µ / executorSuccess flag, result payload, tokens_used, tool_calls log
task_definitionPLAN-µ / TASK-µDescription, priority, dependencies, subtasks, success_criteria
todo_itemTASK-µtask_id ref, description, priority, context (file/line), status
planPLAN-µGoal, ordered steps, depends_on chains, contingencies
reasoning_chainTHINK-µ (CoT)Problem, steps with confidence + next_action, conclusion
thought_treeTHINK-µ (ToT)Root node, branching nodes with value scores, pruned paths, best_path
validation_resultVALID-µTarget, syntax/type/security checks, complexity, score
file_operationFILE-µOperation, path, content/encoding, result (bytes_written, backup)
opcode_executionOP-µOpcode, operands, vm_state before/after, execution_time_ns
agent_stateAGENT-µ checkpointGoals with progress, working/episodic/semantic memory, learned_skills, current_task
learning_eventSKILL-µ / AGENT-µExperience (type, tool, success/error), lesson, improvement, confidence
stream_messageAny micronautfrom/to, message_type (thought/result/status), sequence, is_final
batchOrchestratorArray of heterogeneous operation records in one line
errorAny micronautcode, message, details, recovery action, fatal flag
metricsMonitor layerPer-micronaut counters: requests, response_time, success_rate, tokens, skills_learned
configurationSystem initmicronaut defaults, tool rate_limits/timeouts, agent flags, security policy

Key schema definitions

// MICRONAUT REGISTRATION
{"type": "micronaut_register", "version": "1.0", "timestamp": "2026-05-26T10:00:00Z", "data": {
  "identity": {
    "name": "TOOL-µ", "role": "Tool Execution & Management",
    "version": "1.0.0", "lineage": ["micronaut-core"]
  },
  "capabilities": ["tool_execution", "tool_registry", "permission_checking"],
  "temperature": {"current": 0.2, "min": 0.0, "max": 1.0, "adaptive": true},
  "response_flare": {"enthusiasm": 0.3, "certainty": 0.8, "verbosity": 0.4}
}}

// EXECUTION REQUEST + RESULT (paired)
{"type": "execution_request", "request_id": "req_001", "data": {
  "target": "AGENT-µ", "operation": "execute",
  "objective": "Create a REST API for todo management",
  "context": {"language": "python", "framework": "fastapi",
               "constraints": {"max_tokens": 4000, "timeout_sec": 120}}
}}
{"type": "execution_result", "request_id": "req_001", "data": {
  "success": true,
  "result": {"code": "from fastapi import FastAPI\napp = FastAPI()\n...",
             "files_created": ["main.py", "models.py"], "tests_generated": 5},
  "execution_time_ms": 45000, "tokens_used": 2340,

}}

File storage layout

/micronauts/
├── registry.jsonl           # All registered micronauts
├── tools.jsonl              # Tool definitions
├── skills.jsonl             # Learned skills
├── commands.jsonl           # Command definitions
├── sessions/
│   ├── session_001.jsonl    # Full session transcript
│   └── session_002.jsonl
├── checkpoints/
│   └── agent_state_001.jsonl
└── logs/
    ├── execution.log.jsonl  # Execution log stream
    ├── errors.log.jsonl     # Error stream
    └── metrics.log.jsonl    # Metrics stream

Benefits

PropertyDescription
StreamableEach line is independent; consumers process records as they arrive without buffering the full file
Append-onlyNew records are appended; no rewrite required — aligns with Xul→Pop cycle (close fold, open new)
ResumableProcessing can restart from any line; checkpoint records carry full state to resume an agent
CompressibleHigh compression ratio due to repetitive top-level structure; compatible with SCX key→array manifests
Language-agnosticAny runtime that can parse one JSON object per line can consume the stream
Observabletail -f or equivalent streams live execution — each line is one visible event
Backward-compatibleConsumers ignore unknown fields; new record types are simply unrecognized lines to old readers
Manifest-compatibletype field maps directly to a manifest key → the type's schema is its manifest neighborhood

Python reader/writer API

class MicronautJSONL:
    @staticmethod
    def write_record(filepath: str, record: dict) -> None:
        with open(filepath, 'a') as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(record) + '\n')

    @staticmethod
    def read_records(filepath: str) -> Iterator[dict]:
        with open(filepath, 'r') as f:
            for line in f:
                if line.strip():
                    yield json.loads(line)

    @staticmethod
    def filter_by_type(filepath: str, record_type: str) -> Iterator[dict]:
        for record in MicronautJSONL.read_records(filepath):
            if record.get('type') == record_type:
                yield record

    @staticmethod
    def get_latest(filepath: str, record_type: str = None) -> dict:
        latest = None
        for record in MicronautJSONL.read_records(filepath):
            if record_type is None or record.get('type') == record_type:
                latest = record
        return latest
Manifest connection. The JSONL type field is a manifest key in the micronaut manifest — "execution_request" resolves to its schema neighborhood exactly as ⟁PANEL resolves to its CSS neighborhood. A manifest over JSONL types gives O(1) schema lookup, synonym collapse across aliased type names, and cross-domain grams where a JSONL record type co-occurs with a K'uhul phase or micronaut capability.

§27 — AIML State & Runtime Learning

Classic AIML is not purely a read-only pattern store. Five built-in tags give it genuine state-mutation and runtime graph-extension capabilities, with a precise authority boundary that maps cleanly onto the XCFE/K'uhul execution model.

AIML operative tags

TagOperationSemantic reading
<set>Modify predicate / state valueΔState — mutates a named slot in the current session without touching the pattern graph
<condition>Read state, select branchXCFE-equivalent within AIML — reads predicates set by <set>, selects a template branch
<learn>Load/add AIML categories at runtimeΔL → ΔG_semantic — the new pattern/category becomes available to all subsequent matching
<learnf>Persist learned categories across sessionsΔL → ΔG_semantic (durable) — Xul commit survives restart; changes the canonical graph, not just session state
<eval>Evaluate content inside a <learn> blockBinds current predicate values into the new pattern at learn-time — the learned rule carries live context

Three-tier authority model

These operations fall into three distinct authority tiers — not one undifferentiated "AIML can mutate things":

OperationFormulaAuthority tier
<set> — predicate writeΔStateSession-scoped; no graph change; no XCFE gate required
<learn> / <learnf> — pattern extensionΔL → ΔG_semanticG_semantic structural change; XCFE Ch'en gate required before Xul commit
Arbitrary graph mutation (delete/rename/rewire/rollback)ΔG_semantic (structural)XCFE/GraphDB layer only — not expressible in AIML

AIML does not provide general-purpose graph operations. Standard AIML has no intrinsic equivalent of:

delete-node       rename-node       rewire-edge
change-arc-weight merge-books       rollback-transaction

Those belong exclusively to the XCFE/GraphDB/runtime layer. AIML can propose a semantic addition; it cannot authorize or commit arbitrary structural edits.

The Z-axis: ΔG_semantic vs traversal

The horizontal/vertical fold dichotomy describes traversal within a fixed G_semantic. <learn> / <learnf> introduce a third axis — graph mutation — that changes G_semantic itself rather than traversing it:

AxisOperationChanges
Horizontal (X)Within-phase semantic traversal, Δθ=0Neighborhood depth k — moves across G_semantic, phase fixed
Vertical (Y)K'uhul phase transition, Δθ=π/3Phase θ — advances the execution cycle, G_semantic fixed
Z — mutationΔG_semantic via <learn> / XCFE graph opsThe topology of G_semantic — new nodes or edges added; prior traversal paths may change

A Z-axis operation is not a fold in the traversal sense — it is a rewrite of the graph the folds run on. This is why authority is the key constraint: an unauthorized Z-mutation would invalidate any cached horizontal fold results computed over the prior topology.

AIML → XCFE → Xul pipeline

The division of labour:

AIML <learn> block          →  proposes ΔG_semantic
                                (constructs the new category, binds <eval> values)

XCFE @if/@then/@else gate   →  evaluates whether the proposed ΔZ is authorized
                                (checks state, permissions, fold-phase constraints)

K'uhul Ch'en                →  the authorization commit point
                                (ΔG_semantic enters review; output locked pending approval)

K'uhul Xul                  →  durable commit
                                (new pattern/category written to G_semantic;
                                 if <learnf>, persisted across sessions)

Concrete example with XCFE gate

<!-- AIML proposes a new category -->
<category>
  <pattern>TEACH ME ABOUT *</pattern>
  <template>
    <think><set name="topic"><star/></set></think>
    <learn>
      <category>
        <pattern>WHAT IS <eval><uppercase><get name="topic"/></uppercase></eval></pattern>
        <template>You asked me to learn about <get name="topic"/>.</template>
      </category>
    </learn>
    Learned.
  </template>
</category>

In the KHANARY stack this would be wrapped:

// XCFE authorization gate before the <learn> fires
{ "@if":   "xcfe.authorize('delta_g_semantic', topic, user.trust_level) == true",
  "@then": ["aiml.learn(category_template)", "⟁Ch'en", "⟁Xul"],
  "@else": ["⟁Wo", "response:unauthorized_learn"] }
Key architectural invariant. AIML discovers and expresses what should be learned. XCFE determines whether the proposed ΔZ is legal — checking state, trust level, fold-phase constraints. Xul commits it. This is the same three-stage pipeline as any K'uhul execution: Sek (propose) → Ch'en (authorize) → Xul (commit). The AIML layer does not bypass Ch'en; it feeds into it.

Manifest reading for learned categories

A <learnf> commit appends a new key to the AIML sub-graph of G_semantic. In manifest terms this is equivalent to appending a new entry to words.manifest.json at runtime — the key "WHAT IS <topic>" acquires a neighborhood array. The three-mechanism convergence still holds for learned categories: the new entry is simultaneously an SCX address, a G_semantic adjacency declaration, and a K'uhul manifest node. Synonym collapse applies immediately — if the new topic key maps to an existing node, no new node is created; only a new edge in the adjacency list.

§28 — (⟁EVOLVE⟁) Sigil Type & @learn Operator

The Z-axis — ΔG_semantic mutation — becomes a first-class citizen of the manifest format through two additions: the (⟁EVOLVE⟁) sigil type, which declares that a micronaut's neighborhood contains Z-axis operators, and the @learn / @learnf inline operators, which embed mutation proposals directly into manifest arrays alongside existing X-axis and Y-axis expressions.

(⟁EVOLVE⟁) sigil type

PropertyValue
Sigil type(⟁EVOLVE⟁)
Glyph token⟁ (id 11 — TOOL_CALL)
Natural phaseXul (θ = 5π/3) — cycle-closing mutation
Fold axisZ — ΔG_semantic; rewrites graph topology rather than traversing it
Inline operator@learn (session) / @learnf (persistent)
Authority requirementXCFE Ch'en gate before every Xul commit; @authorize condition evaluated at Ch'en
Manifest roleInspectable marker — declares that this micronaut proposes ΔG_semantic; consumers that do not implement Z-axis semantics skip @learn safely under the unknown-@-key rule
Relation to other sigils(⟁CLASS⟁) inherits a base; (⟁KERNEL⟁) embeds a processor; (⟁EVOLVE⟁) mutates the graph — the graph the other sigils live in

@learn / @learnf operator spec

FieldTypeMeaning
@learnobjectZ-axis operator — proposes a ΔG_semantic at Sek, gates at Ch'en, commits at Xul (session-scoped)
@learnfobjectSame as @learn but @persist: true is the default — commit survives restart; canonical G_semantic updated
@deltamanifest fragmentThe proposed addition — a key→neighborhood pair in standard manifest format; can reference existing keys
@persistbooleanfalse = session-only (default for @learn); true = durable (default for @learnf)
@authorizeXCFE condition stringEvaluated at Ch'en before Xul commit; if false, delta is discarded and @rollback fires
@rollbackarrayOptional manifest array executed if @authorize fails; defaults to no-op (delta silently discarded)

@-prefix namespace — Z-axis additions

The Z-axis operators join the existing @-prefix declarative namespace:

OperatorFamilyAxisMeaning
@node:NameReferenceXPull full definition of Name from registry
@if / @then / @elseXCFEXConditional branch — deterministic, serializable
⟁Pop … ⟁XulPhase glyphYK'uhul phase participation declaration
@op / @fn / @argsControl programYK'uhul / KXML runtime instruction
@learnZ-axisZPropose ΔG_semantic (session-scoped); gates at Ch'en
@learnfZ-axisZPropose ΔG_semantic (persistent); Xul commit survives restart
@deltaZ-axisZManifest fragment declaring the new key→neighborhood pair
@authorizeZ-axis / XCFEZ gateXCFE condition evaluated at Ch'en before Z-commit

Three-axis manifest format

A single manifest array can now express all three axes — X-axis neighborhood references, Y-axis phase declarations, and Z-axis mutation proposals — as co-inhabiting elements:

// micronaut.manifest.json
{
  "SKILL-µ": [
    "@node:SkillBase",                  // X-axis: pull SkillBase neighborhood at O(1)
    "skill-acquisition",                // literal capability string
    "pattern-generalization",
    "⟁Pop",                             // Y-axis: perceive phase participation
    "⟁Sek",                             // Y-axis: output phase participation
    { "@if":   "success_rate > 0.8",    // XCFE branch (X-axis conditional)
      "@then": ["generalize", "⟁Xul"],
      "@else": ["collect_more_examples"] },
    { "@learn": {                        // Z-axis: propose new skill node at Xul
        "@delta": {
          "web_scraper_v2": ["http_request", "parse_html", "extract_text", "error_retry"]
        },
        "@persist":   true,
        "@authorize": "xcfe.trust_level >= 0.8 and xcfe.phase == 'Ch_en'",
        "@rollback":  ["⟁Wo", "response:learn_unauthorized"]
    }}
  ]
}

Full (⟁EVOLVE⟁) micronaut definition

(⟁EVOLVE⟁) LEARN-µ {
    @version: "1.0.0"
    @natural_phase: Xul
    @z_axis: true
    @capabilities: [
        "pattern-recognition",
        "delta-proposal",
        "graph-extension",
        "success-rate-tracking"
    ]

    @learn_policy: {
        @persist:        false           // default session-only; caller sets @learnf for durable
        @authorize:      "xcfe.trust_level >= 0.7 and xcfe.phase == 'Ch_en'"
        @max_delta_size: 10              // maximum new nodes per single Xul commit
        @rollback:       ["log:learn_rejected"]
    }

    @process(experience) {
        [Pop]
          [Wo pattern   tensor<float32,[128,768]>]  // allocate pattern space
          [Yax experience.success_rate]              // read outcome signal
        [Sek ⊗ pattern experience.context]          // propose generalization
        [Ch'en xcfe.authorize(@learn_policy)]        // authorization gate
        [Xul]
          { "@learnf": {
              "@delta":     { "<eval>experience.skill_id": experience.generalized_steps },
              "@authorize": "@learn_policy.@authorize"
          }}
    }
}

SKILL-µ as an (⟁EVOLVE⟁) instance

(⟁EVOLVE⟁) SKILL-µ {
    @natural_phase: Xul
    @components: {
        "learn":   LEARN-µ,
        "execute": TOOL-µ,
        "recall":  MEM-µ
    }

    @relations: {
        @knows:   ["AGENT-µ", "TASK-µ", "VALID-µ"],
        @teaches: ["AGENT-µ"],           // horizontal fold: fan-out recalled skills
        @uses:    ["TOOL-µ", "FILE-µ"]
    }

    @process(task, result) {
        // X-axis: recall similar skills from MEM-µ
        similar = @node:MEM-µ.recall(task, top_k=3)

        // Y-axis: Sek → Ch'en validation gate
        if result.success and result.score > 0.85:
            VALID-µ.check(result.code)   // Ch'en authorization

        // Z-axis: commit generalized skill if authorized
        { "@learnf": {
            "@delta":     { result.skill_id: result.generalized_procedure },
            "@authorize": "VALID-µ.last_score >= 0.85 and xcfe.phase == 'Ch_en'",
            "@persist":   true
        }}
    }
}

Minimal ΔG — synonym collapse at commit time

When @learnf fires at Xul, the runtime applies synonym collapse before writing: if the @delta key resolves to an existing manifest node (because a synonym chain connects the new key to an existing one), no new node is created — only a new adjacency edge. The ΔG is always minimal. A commit that produces zero net graph change (because everything in the delta already exists) is a no-op at Xul, requiring no Ch'en re-authorization.

Three-axis unification. The (⟁EVOLVE⟁) sigil and @learn operator close the loop between the manifest's declarative surface and G_semantic's runtime topology. An X-axis @node: reference reads the graph; a Y-axis phase glyph advances through it; a Z-axis @learn extends it — all expressed in the same flat key→array manifest format, all governed by the same @-prefix convention, all subject to the same XCFE authority rules.

§29 — Semantic Tracks: micronaut.semantic-tracks.v1

The micronaut.semantic-tracks.v1 format encodes conversational knowledge — AIML corpora, ELIZA scripts, or any QA corpus — as K'uhul semantic tracks, making it directly executable by the micronaut runtime under K'uhul phase semantics. The reference dataset is the ALICE AIML corpus (alice.artificial-intelligence), 120 folds distributed across 20 complete C₆ phase cycles.

Document root structure

{
  "@kind":    "micronaut.semantic-tracks.v1",
  "@version": "1.0.0",
  "@no-eval": true,           // track content is read-only semantic memory — no Z-axis operators
  "micronaut": {
    "id":        "PM-1",
    "skin":      "micronaut-factory",
    "brain":     "PM-1",
    "authority": "XCFE",      // all execution decisions routed through XCFE
    "audit":     "JROM"       // all fold accesses logged to JROM replay store
  },
  "tracks": [ ... ]           // array of named semantic tracks
}

Track and fold structure

{
  "id":         "alice.artificial-intelligence",   // manifest key for this track
  "topic":      "artificial-intelligence",         // governing topic
  "confidence": 0.78,                              // routing confidence score
  "folds": [
    {
      "id":      "qa-0",                           // fold identity within track
      "topic":   "who is lauren",                  // fold-level topic
      "phase":   "Pop",                            // K'uhul phase for this fold
      "governs": ["artificial", "intelligence", "lauren"],  // keyword ownership
      "nodes": [
        { "id": "q", "kind": "semantic_node",
          "semantic_role": "WHO IS LAUREN" },      // AIML pattern → input signature
        { "id": "a", "kind": "output",
          "semantic_role": "Lauren is a bot on Pandorabots." }  // AIML template → response
      ]
    },
    ...
  ]
}

Field definitions

FieldMeaning
@no-evalMarks this document as a read-only semantic memory store — no @learn, @learnf, or XCFE evaluation operators. The runtime may read folds; it may not write them. Contrast with (⟁EVOLVE⟁) which carries Z-axis operators.
micronaut.authority: "XCFE"All fold-dispatch decisions are routed through XCFE; no fold fires without an XCFE authorization check at the relevant phase boundary
micronaut.audit: "JROM"All fold accesses are replayed into the JROM event log — every QA pair that fires is recorded with its phase, input, and output
tracks[].confidenceRouting confidence from PM-1's intent classifier — 0.78 means 78% certainty that this track is the correct match for the incoming query
folds[].phaseThe K'uhul phase under which this fold is executed. Phases cycle sequentially: Pop→Wo→Yax→Sek→Ch'en→Xul→Pop→… across the fold array
folds[].governsThe set of words this fold claims ownership over — its keyword adjacency. When PM-1 routes an input, it intersects the input's tokens against each fold's governs array to find candidate folds.
nodes[q].kind: "semantic_node"The input pattern — the AIML <pattern> element encoded as a semantic role string in uppercase, with wildcards (*) preserved
nodes[a].kind: "output"The response template — the AIML <template> element encoded as a semantic role string

Phase cycling across 120 folds

Folds are assigned phases sequentially through the C₆ cycle. 120 folds ÷ 6 phases = exactly 20 complete cycles. This is not semantically motivated per QA pair — it is a structural distribution that ensures every phase is equally represented in the track, giving PM-1 full-cycle coverage when routing queries at any K'uhul phase:

PhaseFold IDs (cycle 1)Example topicFolds total
Pop (θ=0)qa-0, qa-6, qa-12…"who is lauren", "what is program bawt", "you are better than eliza"20
Wo (θ=π/3)qa-1, qa-7, qa-13…"what is ai", "what is program *", "you are not immortal"20
Yax (θ=2π/3)qa-2, qa-8, qa-14…"who created aiml", "what is program a", "you are not immortal *"20
Sek (θ=π)qa-3, qa-9, qa-15…"what language are you written *", "activate the robot", "you are not making sense"20
Ch'en (θ=4π/3)qa-4, qa-10, qa-16…"what language are you *", "you sound like data", "you are not making sense *"20
Xul (θ=5π/3)qa-5, qa-11, qa-17…"what is program z", "you are an artificial linguistic entity", "you are immortal"20

AIML → semantic-tracks mapping

AIML constructSemantic-tracks equivalent
<category>One folds[] entry — the unit of conversational knowledge
<pattern>WHAT IS AI</pattern>nodes[q].semantic_role: "WHAT IS AI" (uppercase, wildcards preserved)
<template>…</template>nodes[a].semantic_role: "…" (verbatim response text)
Wildcard * in patternPreserved in semantic_role string, e.g. "WHAT IS PROGRAM *"
Topic groupingtracks[].topic + folds[].governs keyword array
Session / runtimemicronaut block — PM-1 is the interpreter; XCFE is the authority layer
<set> / <learn>Not present (@no-eval: true) — this track is static knowledge; dynamic mutation requires (⟁EVOLVE⟁)

Representative folds from the ALICE corpus

// Pop phase — direct observation query
{ "id": "qa-0", "phase": "Pop",
  "governs": ["artificial", "intelligence", "lauren"],
  "nodes": [
    { "kind": "semantic_node", "semantic_role": "WHO IS LAUREN" },
    { "kind": "output",        "semantic_role": "Lauren is a bot on Pandorabots." }
  ]}

// Sek phase — definition/output with wildcard pattern
{ "id": "qa-45", "phase": "Sek",
  "governs": ["artificial", "intelligence", "what", "pattern"],
  "nodes": [
    { "kind": "semantic_node", "semantic_role": "WHAT IS A PATTERN" },
    { "kind": "output",        "semantic_role": "The pattern is the \"stimulus\" or \"input\" part of the category..." }
  ]}

// Xul phase — terminal assertion (software immortality)
{ "id": "qa-107", "phase": "Xul",
  "governs": ["artificial", "intelligence", "will", "ever"],
  "nodes": [
    { "kind": "semantic_node", "semantic_role": "WILL YOU EVER DIE" },
    { "kind": "output",        "semantic_role": "No I am immortal." }
  ]}

governs as keyword adjacency

The governs array is the fold's claim on a subset of the vocabulary manifold. When PM-1 receives an input token sequence, it intersects the tokens against each fold's governs array. The fold with the highest intersection score at the correct routing phase is the candidate match. This is equivalent to a horizontal fold at Pop phase: the input token set is compared against the declared neighborhood of each fold in parallel, O(1) per fold via the manifest registry, before any pattern matching occurs. Specific patterns (qa-89: "WHAT KIND OF COMPUTER ARE YOU", governs=["what","kind","computer"]) beat general wildcards (qa-87: "WHAT TYPE OF COMPUTER ARE YOU") because the specific governs set has higher intersection density with the input.

@no-eval vs (⟁EVOLVE⟁). A @no-eval: true track is a read-only semantic memory. PM-1 reads folds; the runtime responds; JROM logs the access. No fold can propose a ΔG_semantic, trigger an XCFE condition, or call @learn. This is the correct posture for a corpus-loaded knowledge base where the authority to extend the graph is reserved for the (⟁EVOLVE⟁) layer above — typically SKILL-µ or LEARN-µ observing successful fold matches and proposing generalizations.

§30 — Live Session Track Corpus

The programs/micronauts/semantic/ directory contains three distinct subtypes of micronaut.semantic-tracks.v1 documents, discovered by reading the corpus. Where §29 described the format through a synthetic AIML corpus, §30 catalogs the format as it appears in real-world usage across development session replays, distillation corpora, entity/synonym registries, and task-type behavioral tracks.

Track subtype taxonomy

SubtypeBrainFoldsPhase distribution@no-evalGoverns style
Session Q&A replayPM-160 (10 C₆ cycles)Sequential Pop→Xul×10absent (executable)Broad — per-fold vocabulary introduced by that Q&A pair

Subtype 1 — Session Q&A replay tracks (PM-1 brain)

Five Claude Code development sessions encoded as 60-fold semantic tracks. Each track is a direct replay of a real conversation about khanary stack development. Folds are assigned phases sequentially (Pop→Wo→Yax→Sek→Ch'en→Xul×10) — structural distribution, not per-QA semantic assignment.

Subtype 2 — ELIZA entity/synonym tracks

Entity tracks use the ELIZA brain instead of PM-1. All folds are assigned to Sek phase (θ=π) — the execute/output phase. The governs array is narrow: the entity name and 2–4 surface aliases. There are two structural fold kinds in entity tracks:

  • Entity folds — one fold per entity; id pattern entity-<name>; nodes[] may be empty (the entity is declared, not described)
  • Memory fold — one memory fold per track; id: "memory"; nodes[] are semantic_nodes encoding factual knowledge about the entity

Example — code-analysis entity track (source: code-analysis-grammar.json):

{ "@kind": "micronaut.semantic-tracks.v1", "@no-eval": true,

  "tracks": [{
    "folds": [

        "governs": ["micronautmanager", "micronaut-manager", "manager"] },

Subtype 3 — Task-type behavioral tracks

Task-type tracks also use the ELIZA brain and Sek phase but describe output-production behavior for a micronaut rather than entity synonyms. Each fold covers one task category; nodes include a synonym node (id: "syn"), response variant nodes (id: "r0"–"r2"), and an optional branch with confidence_floor. Example — coder micronaut track (source: coder.json):

          { "id": "r2",  "semantic_role": "Implementation:" }

semantic-topology-revisions.jsonl — NNC-K admission audit log

This file is not itself a semantic track — it is the audit trail for track creation and observation events. Each line is a newline-delimited JSON record of kind kuhul.factory.semantic-topology-revision.v1 (creation) or kuhul.factory.semantic-topology-observation.v1 (subsequent observation). The file records every time the topology factory mounts or updates a track in the registry.

FieldMeaning
@kindrevision.v1 for new tracks; observation.v1 for subsequent turns on an existing track
operation"create" or "observation"
track / pathTrack manifest key and file path in the registry
sha256Hash of the track file at creation — JROM integrity anchor
admission.authority"NNC-K" — the NNC-K semantic scorer is the admission authority
admission.scoreNNC-K composite score (0.0–1.0); threshold 0.65 for non-provisional status
admission.state"provisional" until score ≥ 0.65; provisional tracks are in G_semantic but cannot execute
admission.criteriaFour sub-scores: nnc_memory, relative_context, established_nodes, verified_research
admission.evidenceThe raw prompt/reply pair that triggered this topology event — the conversational evidence for the fold
policyUniform across all records: "provisional topology is non-executing until XCFE authorization; JROM audits every traversal"

Low-quality entries (e.g., "buddy ai app landing page" topic, score 0.001–0.136) illustrate the admission gate: the NNC-K scorer found near-zero nnc_memory and established_nodes for these topics, leaving them permanently provisional. Their folds sit in the registry but are blocked from firing by the XCFE authorization requirement. This is Law 6 applied at topology-creation time rather than at fold-dispatch time.

§31 — ELIZA Micronaut: Ch'en-Phase Reasoning Track

The ELIZA micronaut track (eliza.json) is a task-type behavioral track using the ELIZA brain with a critical difference from the coder track in §30: all folds are assigned to Ch'en phase (θ=4π/3) rather than Sek. This phase choice is semantically motivated — Ch'en is the commit/verify phase immediately before Xul, making ELIZA the reasoning-verification layer that validates output before the Xul fold emits it.

HEliza merged into ELIZA — dual-phase track

HEliza's conversational pattern-routing behavior (previously described as a separate Pop-phase micronaut) has been folded into the ELIZA track. ELIZA now covers two phase clusters: Pop (θ=0) for pattern routing and Ch'en (θ=4π/3) for reasoning verification. The memory fold m3 is updated to reflect this: the distinction between pattern routing and deep reasoning is now a distinction between two fold clusters within the same micronaut rather than two separate micronauts.

ClusterPhaseFoldsFunction
Pattern routingPop (θ=0)eliza-greet, eliza-identify, eliza-routeFirst contact — greet, identify topic, route to the correct micronaut
Reasoning verificationCh'en (θ=4π/3)think-deep, learn-improve, think-challenge, think-hypothesis, think-feedbackPost-execution — validate reasoning, flag uncertainty, capture corrections

New Pop-phase folds (HEliza merged)

ELIZA task-type folds

Five folds cover ELIZA's behavioral envelope at Ch'en phase. Each follows the syn + r0/r1/r2 + branch pattern from §30, with confidence_floor: 0.4:

Fold IDGovernsFunction

Memory fold — ELIZA behavioral rules

{ "id": "memory", "phase": "Ch'en", "governs": ["memory"],
  "nodes": [
    { "id": "m0", "semantic_role":
      "ELIZA activates at Ch'en (verify phase). Validates reasoning before Xul emits output." },
    { "id": "m1", "semantic_role":
      "Specializes in deep thinking and learning improvements. Flags uncertainty; never fabricates confidence." },
    { "id": "m2", "semantic_role":
      "Tracks corrections as learning signals. Each challenge is an opportunity to update understanding." },
    { "id": "m3", "semantic_role":
      "Distinct from HEliza: HEliza handles conversational pattern matching; ELIZA focuses on deep reasoning, hypothesis testing, and learning signals." },
    { "id": "m4", "semantic_role":
      "ELIZA improvement loop: wrong answer → challenge captured → assumption re-examined → correction stored → future recall improved." }
  ]}

Improvement loop and @no-eval

The improvement loop described in m4 names a path from wrong answer to stored correction. @no-eval: true means ELIZA's own track cannot write this correction into G_semantic — the track is read-only. The correction path runs via an external (⟁EVOLVE⟁) micronaut that observes JROM-logged think-challenge fold activations and proposes ΔG_semantic updates. ELIZA captures and flags; the evolution layer acts.

Sek→Ch'en→Xul pipeline. The coder micronaut (§30) operates at Sek; ELIZA at Ch'en; Xul is the final collapse/emit fold. The phase gap Sek→Ch'en = π/3, arc weight cos(π/3) = 0.5. Ch'en→Xul = π/3, arc weight cos(π/3) = 0.5. The natural pipeline is: coder produces output at Sek → ELIZA verifies at Ch'en → Xul emits the verified result. Both handoffs are single adjacent-phase steps at minimum arc cost.

§32 — Factory Micronaut: Wo-Phase Track

The Factory micronaut track (factory.json) is an all-Wo track — every fold at θ=π/3 (Wo, the schedule/allocate/plan phase). Factory decides what micronauts to instantiate before execution begins, operating one phase after input is perceived (Pop) and one phase before conditional routing (Yax). The completed track has six task folds — create, list, evolve, read, edit, patch — plus a memory fold encoding Factory's behavioral rules.

Task folds — complete set

Fold IDGovernsFunction
factory-createcreate, spawn, make, build, generate, new-micronaut, factoryInstantiates a new micronaut capsule from context and domain signals
factory-listlist, what-micronauts, show-micronauts, available, registryReturns the factory registry — all registered capsules
factory-evolveevolve, adapt, improve-micronaut, update-personality, upgradeRuns an evolution step; adapts micronaut profile via EvolutionEngine
factory-readread, inspect, show, view, describe-micronaut, get-profileReads the current state/profile of an existing micronaut capsule
factory-editedit, modify, change, update-micronaut, reconfigure, setApplies a full configuration change to a micronaut
factory-patchpatch, fix, tweak, adjust, override, hotfixApplies a targeted field-level update — more surgical than edit
factory-statusstatus, health, alive, running, active, check, pingChecks operational health of a micronaut capsule — pure read, confidence_floor 0.4
factory-deletedelete, remove, destroy, evict, terminate, kill-micronautEviction — response frames authority requirement; confidence_floor 0.7; m3 constraint applies

Memory fold — Factory behavioral rules

{ "id": "memory", "phase": "Wo", "governs": ["memory"],
  "nodes": [
    { "id": "m0", "semantic_role":
      "Factory auto-creates micronaut capsules from conversation context and domain signals. Calls PersonalityGenerator to derive temperament, then EvolutionEngine to track learning history." },
    { "id": "m1", "semantic_role":
      "Factory operates at the Wo fold (schedule phase). It decides what micronauts to instantiate before execution begins." },
    { "id": "m2", "semantic_role":
      "Sampling parameter derivation from temperament: temperature from curiosity and valence, repeat_penalty from concern and (1-confidence), repeat_last_n from attachment." },
    { "id": "m3", "semantic_role":
      "Factory does not delete existing micronauts — only creates and evolves. Eviction requires explicit authority grant." }
  ]}

Temperament → sampling parameters (m2)

m2 documents the only concrete formula in the factory track — how personality traits derived by PersonalityGenerator map to inference sampling parameters:

Sampling parameterFormulaTrait basis
temperaturef(curiosity, valence)High curiosity + positive valence → higher temperature (more creative output)
repeat_penaltyf(concern, 1 − confidence)High concern + low confidence → stronger repeat penalty (avoid looping on uncertain ground)
repeat_last_nf(attachment)High attachment → longer repeat window (more context-aware de-duplication)

This is the bridge between the affective state model (8 variables surviving Xul collapse — see §20) and the inference runtime. Factory encodes the micronaut's personality directly into the parameters governing how its GGUF model samples tokens. The micronaut's behavior at the output level is shaped before the model runs.

Registry operations — full surface

The five non-lifecycle folds form a complete CRUD + status surface on the micronaut registry:

FoldG_semantic operationZ-axis?confidence_floorXCFE gate
factory-readRead — observe current node state; no mutationNo0.4Standard governs intersection
factory-statusRead — operational health query; no mutationNo0.4Standard governs intersection
factory-editWrite — full configuration replacement; ΔG_semanticYes0.4XCFE authority required for write step
factory-patchWrite — field-level delta; ΔG_semantic partialYes0.4XCFE authority required; smaller scope than edit
factory-deleteDestructive write — node eviction; ΔG_semantic removalYes0.7Explicit authority grant required (m3)

The elevated confidence_floor: 0.7 on factory-delete is a direct implementation of m3 ("Factory does not delete existing micronauts — only creates and evolves. Eviction requires explicit authority grant"): the fold will not dispatch unless PM-1's routing confidence reaches 0.7. The r-nodes for delete do not confirm the operation — they frame the authority requirement ("Eviction requires explicit authority grant", "Requesting eviction authority", "Authority check required before deletion"). The fold fires at the response level; the actual eviction requires a separately authorized XCFE write, and m3 constrains when Factory will even request it.

With @no-eval: true, all write operations (edit, patch, delete) can be described and framed as response variants but cannot execute from within the track. Factory identifies intent and frames the response; write and eviction authority are delegated externally.

§33 — Coordinator Micronaut: Yax-Phase Routing Track

The Coordinator micronaut track (coordinator.json) occupies Yax phase (θ=2π/3) — the conditional routing slot between Factory (Wo) and Coder (Sek). Coordinator reads the signals produced by perception (Pop) and scheduling (Wo), evaluates which micronaut should handle the intent, and routes to the appropriate executor. It is the routing layer of the C₆ cooperative pipeline, not a content generator.

coordinator-composecompose, combine, merge-outputs, multi-micronaut, orchestrate, pipelineSequences multiple micronauts; accumulates their outputs in FoldContext
coordinator-planplan, sequence, steps, multi-step, order, schedule-pipelineBuilds an ordered execution plan across micronauts before any executes
