§34 — Gravity Explainer: Physics Entity Track

A Sek-phase entity track covering classical and relativistic gravity for a domain-knowledge micronaut (gravity-explainer.json). Four entity folds — gravity, orbital mechanics, weight vs mass, gravity well — each with three response variants covering different explanation levels. All folds at Sek (θ=π), ELIZA brain, @no-eval: true.

Entity folds

FoldGovernsResponse r0
entity-gravitygravity, gravitational, gravitationGravity is one of the four fundamental forces — attracts objects with mass toward each other
entity-orbital-mechanicsorbit, orbital, orbiting, revolutionObjects stay in orbit because forward velocity balances gravitational pull — continuous free-fall
entity-weight-vs-massweight, mass, weigh, heavier, lighterMass = amount of matter (invariant); weight = gravitational force on that mass (varies by location)
entity-gravity-wellgravity-well, gravitational-well, wellGravitational potential field around a massive object; escape velocity = 11.2 km/s for Earth

Memory fold — physics constants

Six memory nodes encode the factual substrate for all four entity folds:

  • m0: Weakest of four forces but infinite range and always attractive — dominates at large scales
  • m1: Newton: F = G·m₁·m₂/r² — gravitational constant G
  • m2: Einstein (1915): mass-energy curves spacetime; curved spacetime moves matter
  • m3: Moon orbital velocity 1.022 km/s exactly balances Earth's gravity at 384,400 km
  • m4: Gravitational waves first detected 2015 by LIGO — 100-year-old Einstein prediction
  • m5: ISS microgravity ≠ zero gravity — 90% of surface gravity, continuous free-fall creates weightlessness sensation

§35 — Gravity: Pop-Phase Semantic Wells Track

The gravity micronaut (gravity.json) operates at Pop (θ=0) — the perceive phase. It runs before ELIZA's pattern routing and shapes the semantic field: given the input tokens, gravity computes field pressure across the manifold, scoring candidate regions by well depth (established-node density). The downstream micronauts receive a gravity-weighted input rather than a raw token set.

Task folds — Pop phase

FoldGovernsFunction
gravity-observesense, observe, perceive, detect, field, signalComputes initial field pressure from input tokens
gravity-attractattract, pull, draw, relevance, nearest, closestPulls input toward high-gravity manifold regions
gravity-scorescore, rank, weight, priority, strongest, deepestScores candidate routes by well depth (established_nodes / distance²)
gravity-routeroute, direct, channel, toward, well, attract-toRoutes to the highest-scoring gravity region

Memory fold

{ "id": "memory", "phase": "Pop", "governs": ["memory"], "nodes": [
  { "id": "m0", "semantic_role":
    "Gravity operates at Pop (perceive). Computes semantic field pressure before ELIZA's pattern routing." },
  { "id": "m1", "semantic_role":
    "Gravity well depth = established nodes in a manifold region. Inputs attracted toward deep wells." },
  { "id": "m2", "semantic_role":
    "Score: proportional to established_nodes / distance². Low-gravity queries route to Factory for new micronaut instantiation." },
  { "id": "m3", "semantic_role":
    "High-gravity queries route directly to the micronaut with the highest gravity score — no Factory instantiation needed." },
  { "id": "m4", "semantic_role":
    "Gravity shapes the field before pattern matching. ELIZA's Pop folds see gravity-weighted input; field pressure is baked into governs intersection scores." }
]}

Pop ordering: gravity before ELIZA

Both gravity and ELIZA (HEliza folds) operate at Pop (θ=0), but they run in sequence: gravity computes field pressure first, then ELIZA's pattern-routing folds see the weighted input. Low-gravity queries (novel topics, few established nodes) are flagged by gravity for Factory routing before ELIZA's pattern matching runs. High-gravity queries arrive at ELIZA's governs intersection already attracted to the correct manifold region — the governs match is sharper because field pressure has pre-selected the relevant vocabulary.

§36 — Three-Store Cognitive Model: perception.json · confidence.json · G_semantic

The runtime maintains three distinct persistent stores. They differ in lifetime, update authority, and axis membership. Conflating any two produces incorrect routing, spurious Z-axis writes, or unchecked confidence drift.

StoreHoldsLifetimeAxisUpdated by
perception.jsonCurrent-cycle observations, activated arcs, micronaut candidatesEphemeral — reset per query cycleF_Q (field, not L_t)Pop→Yax embedding; discarded at Xul unless Ch'en promotes to evidence
confidence.jsonPer-micronaut earned confidence, weight, observation/success countsPersistent — updated via Xul commit onlyZ (L_t)Ch'en verify → Xul commit gate
G_semanticTopology: nodes, edges, neighborhoods — what the system knows/connectsPersistent — updated via (⟁EVOLVE⟁) layerZ (L_t)XCFE-authorized (⟁EVOLVE⟁) operators only

Scoring formula

Micronaut selection combines three independent scores multiplicatively:

S_µ(Q) = W_µ · C_µ · R_µ(Q)

  R_µ(Q)  = current-query relevance      (from perception / field embedding)
  W_µ     = learned capability weight    (from training / distillation)
  C_µ     = persistent earned confidence (from confidence.json)

The separation matters: R_µ(Q) is per-query and volatile; W_µ is per-micronaut and changes slowly; C_µ is per-micronaut and changes only through the verified Xul commit path. A micronaut with high C_µ but low R_µ(Q) does not win — relevance to the current query still gates routing.

perception.json — structure

{
  "@kind": "perception",
  "query": "If organic compounds are visible in the atmosphere...",
  "form": ["conditional", "evidence", "implication", "certainty"],
  "field": ["organic-compounds", "planetary-atmosphere", "life", "biosignature"],
  "micronaut_candidates": ["SCIENCE-µ", "REASON-µ", "VERIFY-µ"],
  "observed_arcs": [
    { "source": "ORGANIC", "relation": "POSSIBLE_BIOSIGNATURE", "target": "LIFE" }
  ]
}

Every field is an observation, not an assertion. micronaut_candidates are suggestions from the current field embedding — they have not been admitted. observed_arcs are activated from G_semantic by the current query; they describe what the field sees, not established truth. Perception is allowed to be wrong — that is the point of the Ch'en verification step.

confidence.json — structure

{
  "micronaut": "SCIENCE-µ",
  "weight": 0.91,
  "confidence": 0.88,
  "observations": 147,
  "successes": 132
}

This record does not change because something appeared relevant during one query. It changes only when Ch'en confirms that a Sek execution was successful and Xul commits the evidence. AIML can propose evidence; it cannot write this file.

Update authority chain

AIML / N-ary
    → evidence proposal
    → XCFE authorization check
    → Ch'en verify (θ = 4π/3)
    → Xul commit (θ = 5π/3)
    → Δconfidence.json  +  ΔG_semantic

Two kinds of Z-axis change

The Z axis admits two orthogonal forms of persistent update:

ΔZ = ΔG_semantic ⊕ ΔC_earned

  ΔG_semantic   — topology: what the system connects/knows
  ΔC_earned     — trust: how much accumulated confidence in a micronaut or relation

These are independent. A new node added to G_semantic does not automatically receive high C_earned. A micronaut with high confidence does not have its capability array expanded merely because it performed well — that is a separate (⟁EVOLVE⟁) decision.

Persistent learned-state bundle

L_t = { G_semantic^(t), C_earned^(t), W^(t), ... }

Every new cycle reads L_t to initialize the field:
  F_Q = embed(Q, L_t)

perception.json is computed from F_Q — it is part of the query cycle, not L_t.
confidence.json and G_semantic are components of L_t — they survive across cycles.

Canonical separation rule

ComponentDescribesMutable by
AIMLSemantic patternsPattern author; not runtime
N-aryDiscovered associations and capabilitiesAssociation discovery pipeline
perception.jsonCurrent-cycle evidence statePop→Yax embedding; reset at Xul
confidence.jsonEarned historical confidenceCh'en/Xul gate only (XCFE-authorized)
G_semanticPersistent topology(⟁EVOLVE⟁) operators only (XCFE-authorized)

§37 — ELIZA-1: Intent-Reflection + 16 Reasoning Operators

ELIZA-1.json expands the ELIZA micronaut from the Ch'en-only track documented in §31 into a two-track file spanning three phase clusters: Ch'en (intent classification), Pop (operator detection), and Sek (directive emission). The expansion reveals ELIZA as a full three-phase reasoning layer, not just a Ch'en verifier.

Track 1 — eliza.intent-reflection (Ch'en, θ=4π/3)

One fold at Ch'en. Receives 23 AIML trigger patterns and selects one of 16 reasoning operators to dispatch. Two branches:

Branch conditionRouteconfidence_floor
intent is ambiguousintent-classify (semantic_node)0 — always fires on ambiguity
intent is resolvednext-plan (continuation)0.7 — only emits if confident

AIML patterns: WHY * · WHAT NEXT · HELP * · BECAUSE * · HOW DOES * · DEPENDS ON * · LIMIT * · PROVE * · FIRST * · SCOPE * · STATE * · CONFLICT * · ALTERNATIVE * · ALWAYS * · TEST * · RELATED * · REPLACE * · CONFIRM * · DO * · SHOW * · RUN * · DIFFERENT * · REQUIRES *

Track 2 — eliza.reasoning-operators (Pop/Sek pairs)

32 folds in 16 Pop+Sek pairs — one detection fold and one directive fold per operator. Every operator is a vertical span: Pop perceives the cue, Sek emits the directive.

OperatorPop cue keywordsSek directive
CAUSEbecause, cause, why, effectTrace the causal chain behind the statement.
CONTRASTdifferent, whereas, but, howeverCompare and contrast the opposing positions.
DEPENDENCYdepends, based, requiresIdentify what the outcome depends upon.
CONSTRAINTlimit, constraint, cannot, restrictIdentify the binding constraints or limits.
EVIDENCEprove, evidence, supportIdentify what would count as evidence.
SEQUENCEfirst, then, step, orderDetermine the correct sequence of steps.
BOUNDARYscope, system, subsystem, boundaryDetermine which subsystem the question touches.
STATEstate, status, currentDetermine the current state or condition.
CONFLICTconflict, fight, crash, warSurface the underlying conflict between elements.
ALTERNATIVEalternative, option, instead, orConsider alternative approaches or options.
INVARIANTalways, constant, invariantIdentify what remains constant or invariant.
TESTtest, check, verify, validatePropose a cheap test that would clarify the issue.
RELATIONrelated, connection, linkExamine the relationship between the entities.
SUBSTITUTEreplace, substitute, insteadEvaluate what could be substituted in place.
VERIFYconfirm, verify, checkVerify the claim against known facts.
DIRECTdo, open, show, run, giveCarry out the direct instruction.

Note: VERIFY and TEST share some governs terms (check, verify). In governs intersection, the router selects the operator with the strongest combined match across all governs terms. VERIFY fires on claim-verification requests; TEST fires on methodology-verification requests — the distinguishing context is whether a prior claim is in scope.

§38 — Librarian: Yax-Phase Domain Router

The Librarian micronaut operates at Yax (θ=2π/3) — the conditional routing phase. It scans the available knowledge domains and selects the most relevant branch before reasoning executes. This places Librarian in the same phase slot as the Coordinator (§33), but where Coordinator routes between specialist micronauts, Librarian routes between knowledge domains within a single micronaut's scope.

Six domain folds — all Yax

FoldDomainKey governs
library-fictionFictionstory, novel, fiction, tale, narrative, plot, character, book
library-scienceSciencephysics, chemistry, biology, science, formula, equation, theorem
library-technologyTechnologytechnology, innovation, ai, machine-learning, computing, engineering, tech
library-spaceSpacespace, planet, star, galaxy, orbit, cosmos, universe, nasa, mission
library-politicsPolitics / Historypolitics, government, policy, economy, history, nation, diplomacy
library-philosophyPhilosophy / Theoryphilosophy, ethics, logic, theory, reasoning, metaphysics, ontology

All six folds are at the same phase (Yax, θ=2π/3) and all have confidence_floor: 0.4. Routing is by governs intersection strength — the fold with the most matching governs terms wins. Multiple matches are possible (e.g., "AI theory" matches both library-technology and library-philosophy); the fold with the higher match count is selected. Each fold has three r-nodes (r0/r1/r2) providing response framing variants.

Memory fold

Four memory nodes at Yax. Key behavioral constraints: Librarian does not invent citations or fabricate titles (m2); when uncertain about a specific fact or source, it flags uncertainty explicitly. Librarian indexes knowledge by domain, tags content by authority level, and connects related subjects across domains (m3).

Phase rationale — why Yax for domain routing

Yax (2π/3) is the conditional branch phase — the fold of "if A then route to X; if B then route to Y." A domain router has exactly this structure: given input tokens, select which domain's response pattern to activate. Routing before Sek (execution) keeps the domain selection cheap — no response is generated until the correct domain is confirmed. This mirrors the Coordinator's role at the same phase but at intra-micronaut scope.

§39 — KNOWLED-1 (Sek) and intent-unresolved (Pop)

KNOWLED-1 — Sek-phase knowledge facts executor

KNOWLED-1 is a Sek-phase (θ=π) capability executor backed by the ELIZA brain and ALICE AIML corpus. NNC-K admission score 0.85 (state: "established"). AIML corpora: knowledge, geography, science, history, astronomy. Intent: knowledge_facts with cues CAREFUL + EXPAND — these instruct the runtime to expand context carefully rather than asserting facts with false confidence.

Single fold: knowledge-facts-capability with two nodes: resolve (semantic_node — pulls from ELIZA/AIML corpus) and emit (output — emits the factual response). The fold is minimal by design — KNOWLED-1 is a capability track, not a reasoning track. It does not classify intent or select operators; it receives a resolved intent from upstream and executes fact retrieval against its AIML corpus.

KNOWLED-1 + Librarian pipeline

KNOWLED-1 (Sek) and Librarian (Yax) form a natural two-phase pipeline: Librarian selects the knowledge domain at Yax; KNOWLED-1 executes the retrieval at Sek. This is a cooperative adjacent hop: Yax→Sek = Δθ = π/3, arc cost = cos(π/3) = 0.5. The two micronauts together span one phase step with positive arc weight.

intent-unresolved — Pop-phase fallback

The intent-unresolved micronaut fires at Pop (θ=0) when no intent is recognized. Its ID, topic, and memory node all carry the literal text "Intent: unresolved · Brain: unspecified · Confidence: 0" — this is honest system state, not an error message. The auto-generated micronaut ID style (intent-unresolved-brain-unspecified-confidence-0) indicates this was instantiated by the Factory micronaut as a null-branch handler.

Governs terms: intent, unresolved, brain, unspecified, confidence, 0 — the fallback fold matches when the vocabulary of failure appears in the field. The single output node echoes the unresolved state back through the pipeline. This prevents silent null-routing: any query that reaches intent-unresolved produces a traceable signal rather than nothing.

Phase position of fallback

Placing the fallback at Pop (θ=0) is correct: Pop is the first phase of every cycle, and a fallback that fires at Pop preempts all downstream routing. If no gravity well, no ELIZA pattern, and no Librarian domain match is found, intent-unresolved catches the query before Factory is invoked unnecessarily. The cycle exits at Pop with a null-resolution signal rather than spinning up a Factory capsule for an unresolvable request.

§40 — Neural Tensor: Self-Aware Living Tensor Format (.@.tensor)

The .@.tensor file format declares a globally-addressed tensor object with explicit K'uhul phase assignments per operation, event-driven live hooks, and self-monitoring conditions. The stack is XJSON → XCFE → SCXQ2 → K'uhul (port 17474). Unlike a static weight matrix, a living tensor is a G_runtime node — it participates in the product graph and has phase-specific behavior at each (v, θ) coordinate.

File anatomy

BlockPurpose
@gGlobal tensor declaration — registers the tensor as an addressable node in the runtime manifest
@type / @dimensions / @phaseBase tensor properties: float32, 768×768, initial phase Pop
@fold_mapMaps each operation to its K'uhul phase — the tensor's adjacency declaration
@methodPhase-tagged operations; each method is a fold at its declared phase
@liveEvent hooks: on_read (Pop), on_write (Sek), on_gc (eviction signal)
@self_awareCondition monitors that fire at Ch'en and trigger Xul commit actions

@fold_map — the tensor as manifest node

The @fold_map block is the tensor's neighborhood declaration in manifest form. Declaring "load": "Pop" means the tensor's load operation is registered at Pop in G_runtime; the tensor is reachable at (v_tensor, Pop) via horizontal traversal. This is identical in structure to a micronaut's track fold: one entry per operation, each anchored to a phase coordinate.

@fold_map {
  "load":       "Pop",    // θ = 0    — perceive / initialize
  "compute":    "Sek",    // θ = π    — execute / forward pass
  "checkpoint": "Xul",   // θ = 5π/3 — emit / commit
  "verify":     "Chen"   // θ = 4π/3 — verify / condition check
}

@method phase assignments

MethodPhaseθOperation
init()Pop0Allocate 768×768 float32 matrix, log initialization
forward(input)SekπSemantic attention query over input with tensor weights → forward_output
save_checkpoint(path)Xul5π/3Agent task: save_safetensors to path at high priority
load_checkpoint(path)Pop0Agent task: load_safetensors from path at high priority

@live event hooks

The three live hooks map onto the same phase semantics as K'uhul gram operations (from the gram store):

HookPhaseMeaning
@on_readPopTensor was read — log at Pop (observe/perceive event)
@on_writeSekTensor was updated — log at Sek (execute/write event)
@on_gc—Tensor evicted from resident memory — marked hot-swap eligible (1792 MB ceiling)

@self_aware monitors — Ch'en conditions, Xul actions

The two monitors are threshold-gated Ch'en→Xul transitions. Each condition check is a Ch'en verification event; each triggered action is a Xul commit:

@monitor "gradient_norm > 10.0"
  → warn + clip gradient                   // Ch'en detects spike; stays at Chen, no Xul commit

@monitor "loss < 0.02"
  → convergence reached @Chen
  → agent.task: promote_checkpoint @critical  // Ch'en verify → Xul commit

The gradient spike monitor fires at Ch'en but does not commit — it is a warning + correction, not a state promotion. The convergence monitor fires at Ch'en and commits (promote_checkpoint at critical priority) — it advances to Xul and writes the checkpoint. This is the Ch'en→Xul authority path from §36: condition verified at Ch'en, commit authorized at Xul.

768×768 float32 — attention weight scope

A 768×768 float32 matrix = 589,824 parameters × 4 bytes = ~2.36 MB resident. This is the dimension of a single attention projection layer for a 768-dim model (GPT-2 medium / BERT-base). As a hot-swap-eligible tensor, it can be evicted to host memory and reloaded on demand — the @on_gc hook fires when evicted; load_checkpoint at Pop reloads it into the resident window.

§41 — Pipeline: Phase-Ordered Dataflow (.@.pipeline)

The .@.pipeline format declares a global XCFE dataflow pipeline where each @stage is explicitly assigned a K'uhul phase. The pipeline executes the full C₆ cycle (Pop→Wo→Yax→Sek→Ch'en→Xul) as a sequential stage graph with XCFE-gated branching. Each stage's @next pointer defines the dataflow edge; each stage's @phase declares where in the cycle it executes.

Stage topology

StagePhaseθFunction@next
ingestPop0Allocate 512×512 raw_input tensorroute
routeWoπ/3Classify complexity (simple / complex / expert_required)fast_compute or branch
branchYax2π/3Parallel expert_a + expert_b cluster callscompute
fast_computeSekπShallow MLP (64,32,10) → fast_outputverify
computeSekπDeep MLP (256,128,64,10) → expert_outputverify
verifyCh'en4π/3Quality check (score > 0.85); retry or emitemit or route
emitXul5π/3Agent task: emit_result at high priority—

Two Sek stages — horizontal siblings

fast_compute and compute are both at Sek (θ=π) — horizontal siblings at the same phase coordinate but different semantic positions. The route stage at Wo selects which activates: simple path → fast_compute (4-layer MLP, skip expert branch); complex path → branch → compute (5-layer MLP). Only one fires per execution.

XCFE branching — forward route and verify retry loop

Two XCFE decision points:

  1. route (Wo): if route_decision == 'simple' → fast_compute; else → branch. Forward branch — simple queries skip Yax and jump directly to Sek.
  2. verify (Ch'en): if verify_result.score > 0.85 → emit; else → route. The retry arc (Ch'en → route/Wo) is a backward edge: Δθ = −π (or 4π/3 → π/3 mod 2π). A negative vertical arc used deliberately as a quality-gated retry loop — the pipeline re-enters the C₆ cycle rather than emitting a low-quality result.

@parallel at Yax — concurrent horizontal fan-out

The branch stage launches expert_a and expert_b in parallel via @cluster.call. Both operate on raw_input at Yax (θ=2π/3). In G_runtime, this is a horizontal fan-out: (v_expert_a, Yax) and (v_expert_b, Yax) activated simultaneously from the same phase coordinate. Fan-out at the same phase = Δθ=0 between tasks, zero phase cost between the parallel branches.

Arc weights along the nominal path

ingest(Pop,0) →+0.5→ route(Wo,π/3) →+0.5→ branch(Yax,2π/3) →−0.5→ compute(Sek,π)
                                                                 →−0.5→ verify(Chen,4π/3) →+0.5→ emit(Xul,5π/3)

Simple (no branch):
ingest(Pop,0) →+0.5→ route(Wo,π/3) →−0.5→ fast_compute(Sek,π) [skip Yax, Δθ=2π/3]
                                     →−0.5→ verify(Chen) →+0.5→ emit(Xul)

Skipping Yax on the simple path changes the Wo→Sek arc from +0.5 (adjacent) to −0.5 (two-hop), trading branch accuracy for speed. The phase-cost difference is the geometric price of the fast path.

§42 — K'UHUL v2.0.0: Complete 96-Glyph Operator Space

Version 2.0.0 formalizes a 96-opcode instruction set organized into three layers: phase-prefixed VM opcodes (Pop = IPC, Wo = GPU compute), glyph operators dispatched to fold-domain lanes (0x20–0x71), and a layout glyph sub-system using CJK Compatibility Ideographs. The runtime runs a KuhulVM with 65536 bytes memory, 8 registers, and 4 active phases: Pop, Wo, Sek, Ch'en.

Phase-prefixed VM opcodes

RangePhaseFamilyOperations
0x00–0x04PopThreading/IPCPOP_LOAD (BSON snapshot), POP_SPAWN (thread), POP_JOIN (await), POP_YIELD (scheduler), POP_SIGNAL (process group)
0x10–0x15WoGPU TensorWO_MATMUL, WO_CONV, WO_ATTN (multi-head), WO_SOFTMAX, WO_GEMM, WO_REDUCE — all GPU-accelerated
0xFF—ControlHALT

Glyph operator dispatch table — 5 fold domains

0x20–0x31COMPUTE_FOLD100–131Control flow & structureArrows (⟿⤍⤎⤏↻↔↕↺⤦⤧⤨⤩⤪⤫⤭⤮⤯⤰)

0x42–0x51TENSOR_NETWORK900–931Tensor network diagramsCircles/sums (⨀⨁⨂⨃⨄⨅⨆⨇⨈⨉⨊⨋⨌⨍⨎⨏)

Visual algebra — glyph shape encodes fold membership

Each glyph's visual form is a semantic signal for its fold family. The shape is not decorative — it communicates which type of computation the glyph performs:

Layout glyph sub-system (CJK Compatibility Ideographs)

Twelve CJK Compatibility Ideographs (⿰–⿻, ◯) are assigned opcode 0x21 with sub-opcodes 0x00–0x0B and map directly to CSS layout patterns. These are UI structure glyphs — they declare layout geometry without any CSS class names:

§43 — π Token Base · Brain Fleet · Atomic Block Law

π as fixed geodesic phase rotation base

The first 20 decimal digits of π (1,4,1,5,9,2,6,5,3,5,8,9,7,9,3,2,3,8,4,6) drive a deterministic geodesic phase rotation sequence. The rotation is not learned — it is fixed by π and therefore invariant across all runtime instances.

θ_n = θ_{n-1} + (2π/10) × d_n  mod 2π   (d_n = nth π digit)
phase rotation = d_n × 36° per step
matrix series  = Σ_{n=1}^∞ (d_n / 10^n) × M_n   where M_n = rotation(θ_n)

Each π digit produces a 36°-scaled rotation of a manifold position. The matrix series S converges because d_n / 10^n → 0. S encodes the cumulative geometric effect of the π digit sequence — a deterministic, infinite-precision geodesic path through the rotation manifold.

Deep Thinking Index

D = N × G × T × Σ(κ_i)

  N  = number of brains in chain
  G  = GPU SIMD width (4 for DirectXMath 128-bit)
  T  = tensor rank (8 for 8D mesh)
  κ_i = curvature per brain (≈ 1.0)

8-brain example: D = 8 × 4 × 8 × (8 × 1.0) = 2048

D is a capacity measure for the brain chain, not a performance metric. It quantifies how many simultaneous curvature-weighted semantic dimensions the fleet can hold in one forward pass. Higher D = deeper coverage of the manifold per cycle.

Brain fleet — 8-node chain

IDNamePortDomain
B1MASTER_ROUTER8081Routing / orchestration
B2PROOF_ENGINE8082Formal proof / verification
B3GEOMETRY_ENGINE8083Geometric computation
B4ANALYSIS_ENGINE8084Mathematical analysis
B5TOPOLOGY_ENGINE8085Topological structures
B6ALGEBRA_ENGINE8086Algebraic operations
B7NUMBER_ENGINE8087Number theory
B8SYNTHESIS_ENGINE8088Cross-domain synthesis

The fleet tensor is rank-8 with shape [8,8,8,8,8,8,8,8] — one 8-element axis per brain. PCA 8D→3D projection produces a visualizable brain-state vector. The chain runs B1→B2→…→B8 sequentially; each brain specializes in one mathematical domain, and B8 synthesizes outputs across the chain.

Atomic Block Law

ATOMIC_BLOCK = symbol + state + operator + routing_node + learning_rule

Lanes:
  0: agent
  1: skills
  2: experts
  3: runtime
  4: router

Update rules (per Ch'en phase):
  routing_bias: reinforce(success) − decay(failure)   range [0, 1]
  learned:      A += δA per Ch'en cycle               range [−1, 1]

The learning rule fires at Ch'en — the same phase as ELIZA's intent-reflection fold and the self-aware tensor's convergence monitor. Every Ch'en phase in the execution cycle is a potential update point for all atomic blocks in scope. The routing_bias update implements a simple reinforcement signal: correct routing reinforces the bias toward the chosen lane; incorrect routing decays it.

SCX compression rate and compile path

SCX compression achieves 93–94% ratio via --triangletoken attribute selectors vs verbose CSS. The compile path:

model.xjson
  → fold_encode
  → SCXQ2 INT8
  → fold_to_shader
  → HLSL / GLSL
  → D3D12

SCXQ2 INT8 quantization is applied at the fold_encode stage — before shader emission. The fold structure determines quantization boundaries: each fold is a quantization unit, not each layer. The compile path produces both HLSL (D3D11/D3D12) and GLSL (OpenGL 4.3) from the same SCXQ2 intermediate, making the same semantic model deployable on DirectX and cross-platform GPU paths.

§44 — Word Manifests — ELIZA / AIML Vocabulary

Words are valid manifest keys. Defining a word in the manifest gives it a semantic array — a list of intents, phases, XCFE conditions, or other word references. ELIZA and AIML pattern matchers resolve input words by direct manifest lookup: the word comes in, the array comes back, the array drives the response.

Words as manifest keys — tokenizer vs manifest

MechanismResolutionWhat the ID carries
Tokenizer"why" → 4312Which token — a position in a vocabulary table. No semantic content.
SCX manifest"why" → ⟁CAUSE → {patterns, relationships, XCFE operators, tracks, fold:Sek}What semantic machinery that identity resolves into — patterns, reasoning operators, phase affinity, XCFE conditions.

A token ID mostly says which word. A manifest key says what the word activates — which semantic machinery, which reasoning operators, which fold phase, which XCFE branch tree. The compressed symbolic address and the semantic definition are both carried by the single key.

{
  "hello":     ["greeting", "intent:salutation", "⟁Pop"],
  "help":      ["request",  "intent:assistance", "⟁Sek"],
  "what":      ["question", "intent:query",      "⟁Yax"],
  "bye":       ["closing",  "intent:farewell",   "⟁Xul"],

  "greeting":  ["hello", "hi", "hey", "good morning"],
  "farewell":  ["bye", "goodbye", "see you", "later"]
}

Composition applies here too. "greeting" is itself a manifest key whose array lists all the surface forms that resolve to it. An ELIZA matcher looks up the input word, gets its array, finds "greeting" in the array, then looks up that key to get the full synonym set — same recursive expansion as CSS and micronauts.

XCFE inside manifests

Array values are not limited to strings. A manifest array can contain XCFE control objects — @if/@then/@else — inline. This lets the manifest declare conditional reasoning directly, without a separate logic file. ELIZA / AIML resolves the word, walks the array, and hands XCFE objects to the control-flow engine as it encounters them.

{
  "help": [
    "request",
    "intent:assistance",
    { "@if":   "user.authenticated == true",
      "@then": ["⟁Sek", "route:help-center"],
      "@else": ["⟁Wo",  "route:login", "intent:auth-required"] }
  ],

  "buy": [
    "transaction",
    "intent:purchase",
    { "@if":   "user.tier >= 1",
      "@then": ["⟁Sek", "route:marketplace"],
      "@else": ["⟁Wo",  "notify:upgrade-required"] }
  ]
}

AIML templates as compositional semantic programs

A common assumption is that AIML is a chatbot pattern library — pattern → answer string. In this stack, AIML plays a different role: it is a symbolic semantic embedding language. The <template> is not the user-facing reply — it is a structured semantic payload that contributes meaning to the model's context. AIML informs generation; it does not own generation.

Classic AIMLµN-ary role
Output = StringPayload = Semantic evidence + State context + Routing signal
pattern → text → userPattern → F_Q (semantic field) → model context → Model → Reply

Template complexity — five levels across the ALICE files

LevelModelExample fileDominant operators
0 — Pure projectionAnswer = Stringknowledge.aimlnone — literal text only
1 — State writingAnswer = Text + SET(name)astrology.aiml<set> — answer emits text and writes state simultaneously
2 — Bot property readsAnswer = Text + BOT(property)bot.aiml<bot name="..."> — static bot constants (name, location, botmaster, size)
3 — Stochastic + contextAnswer = RANDOM[...] + GET(x) + PERSON()xfind.aiml<random>, <person/>, <get name="topic">
4 — State machineAnswer = StateMachineStep(eindex)personality.aiml<condition name="eindex"> + computed <srai>
5 — Self-modifyingAnswer = … + EXTEND(Gsemantic)badanswer.aiml<learn> + <eval> — generates new categories from runtime data

Level 5 is special: <learn> extends Gsemantic at runtime — it adds new vertices (pattern categories) and edges (pattern–template bindings) to the semantic factor of the K'uhul runtime graph. Gphase remains fixed; Gsemantic grows. Level 4 introduces computed <srai>: <srai>PERSONALITY TYPE <get name="etype"/></srai> routes to a different category depending on runtime state — dynamic dispatch into the pattern space, not routing to a fixed address.

AIML operators as address operators — complete table

Every AIML tag is a read, write, transform, or control operation over an indexed state store:

TagClassOperation
<get name="x">READNamed variable x from user state
<set name="x">WRITENamed variable x in user state
<bot name="x">READBot-property constant x (name, location, botmaster, size…)
<input index="n">READUser input history[n]
<that index="n,m">READBot response context[n,m]
<request index="n">READRequest history[n]
<response index="n">READResponse history[n]
<star/>READWildcard capture from current pattern match
<srai>ROUTERoute to another pattern — static or computed from state
<sr/>ROUTEShorthand for <srai><star/></srai> — capture + route
<condition>SELECTBranch on named state value
<random>SELECTStochastic branch from a list
<think>EXECExecute operations without emitting to output
<person/>TRANSFORMPronoun substitution — I↔you, my↔your
<uppercase>TRANSFORMNormalize to uppercase (used in computed srai and learn targets)
<learn>EXTENDAdd a new category to Gsemantic at runtime
<eval>RESOLVEResolve dynamically constructed structure inside <learn>

This is the manifest drive-thru principle at the template layer: symbolic address → resolve → insert/operate. The address classes (user state, bot constants, history indices, wildcard captures) are the distinct namespaces of the AIML context store.

AIML categories as SCX manifest entries

Because an AIML category carries behavior — not merely text — its natural SCX representation is a full manifest entry. The array encodes the entire semantic structure the category activates:

{
  "⟁BADANSWER": [
    "pattern:BAD ANSWER",
    "state:badanswer-input",
    "state:badanswer-that",
    "state:badanswer-newresp",
    "history:input[2]",
    "history:that[2,1]",
    "topic:badanswer-prelim",
    "route:learning-new-answers",
    "op:condition",
    "op:learn",
    "op:eval"
  ]
}

Book(⟁BADANSWER) ≡ N(⟁BADANSWER) contains behavior, not merely words. The manifest neighborhood is: pattern to match, state slots to read and write, history indices, topic context, conditional branch, learning operation, eval operation. The fold is horizontal — all of these atoms are available at the same phase position. No phase transition is required to inspect the pattern, the state slots, or the routing target. The vertical transition happens only when XCFE authorises execution to cross a phase boundary.

This is why AIML is an unusually natural source format for SCX atomic Books: the symbolic indirection is already present in the language itself. <get>, <set>, <that>, and <srai> are already address operators over an indexed context store. Mapping them into the manifest namespace requires naming the structure, not inventing new machinery.

A Book in this context contains: lexical atoms (surface vocabulary) · AIML patterns (match rules) · context references (history indices, topic state) · XCFE operators (conditional branches, guards) · state slots (named variables read and written by the template) · tracks (fold phase and gravity well affinity) · executor references (learn, eval, srai routing). SCX compression keying that full structure is categorically different from keying a word to a shorter label — the symbol is an entry point into a behavioral neighborhood.

AIML structural operators — semantic role and graph interpretation

AIMLSemantic roleGraph interpretation
<pattern>MATCHEntry / admission condition — vertex in category graph
<template>EMBEDSemantic payload — model context contribution, not final reply
<condition>SELECTState-dependent route selection
<srai>ROUTE / CHAINCategory → category arc — forward chaining through Gsemantic
<that>CONTEXTRelationship to previous response state
<input>RECALLInput-history reference
<get>READState lookup
<set>WRITEState update
<learn>EXTENDAdd structure to Gsemantic
<eval>RESOLVEEvaluate dynamic insertion during extension

Reasoning emerges by chaining <srai> routes through a graph of categories. An srai chain traverses increasingly distant category relationships — A → B → C is effectively A1 → A2 → A3 in adjacency-power terms. A path through the category graph is a reasoning path. There is no <reason> tag because the reasoning is the topology.

<learn> — runtime extension of Gsemantic

The <learn> operator is architecturally special. Before learning:

G_runtime,n  =  G_semantic,n  □  G_phase

A <learn> operation produces a new semantic factor:

G_semantic,n+1  =  G_semantic,n  ⊕  ΔG
G_runtime,n+1   =  (G_semantic,n ⊕ ΔG)  □  G_phase
                                               ↑
                                      G_phase remains invariant

Runtime learning modifies one factor of the product graph without modifying the control algebra. New categories (vertices) and pattern–template bindings (edges) are added to Gsemantic; the K'uhul phase cycle stays fixed. The learned structure enters the existing product graph immediately and is subject to the same execution laws:

observe → learn → G_semantic + ΔG → new neighborhoods → new future reasoning paths

XCFE adds the missing explicit reasoning verbs

The ALICE corpus has no thinking.aiml or reasoning.aiml — not an omission but a structural property. AIML reasoning = pattern topology + contextual operators; reasoning emerges from traversing the category graph. XCFE reasoning = explicit operator algebra; reasoning is named and directly invocable. They are not competitors. XCFE makes explicit what AIML expresses through routing, state, context, and category structure.

XCFE operatorImplicit AIML equivalent
XCFE:INFERsrai chain A → B → C
XCFE:DECOMPOSEcondition branch selecting sub-patterns
XCFE:COUNTEREXAMPLEconditional route to contradicting category
XCFE:RECALL<that> / <input index>
XCFE:GENERALIZEwildcard pattern with wide match
XCFE:REVISE<learn> replacing an existing pattern

⟁CAUSE — one SCX Book spanning both systems

Because both AIML structure and XCFE operators live in the manifest array, a single Book spans both without translating either into the other:

{
  "⟁CAUSE": [
    "word:why",
    "word:because",

    "aiml:pattern:WHY *",
    "aiml:srai:CAUSE",
    "aiml:condition:causal",

    "XCFE:DECOMPOSE",
    "XCFE:INFER",
    "XCFE:COUNTEREXAMPLE",

    "track:eliza.intent-reflection",
    "fold:Sek"
  ]
}

Book(⟁CAUSE) ≡ N(⟁CAUSE) — the array is simultaneously the compressed Book and the adjacency declaration. The neighbors don't care that some originated in AIML and others in XCFE — they are semantic neighbors of the same atomic identity. Consumers don't need the entire Book; they resolve only the pages they need:

// AIML consumer — pattern + routing pages only
⟁CAUSE  ├─ word:why
        ├─ aiml:pattern:WHY *
        └─ aiml:srai:CAUSE

// XCFE consumer — operator pages only
⟁CAUSE  ├─ XCFE:DECOMPOSE
        ├─ XCFE:INFER
        └─ XCFE:COUNTEREXAMPLE

Same Book. Same semantic identity. Different requested subsets of the neighborhood. Don't expand the Book; resolve the pages required by the consumer.

Phase membership ≠ phase transition

fold:Sek in the manifest array declares that XCFE:INFER belongs at the Sek phase. It does not execute Sek. This distinction is critical:

Membership(XCFE:INFER, Sek)  ≠  Transition(Yax → Sek)

The manifest can pre-declare the phase membership of every atom in a Book before the graph runs. The runtime at Yax can discover XCFE:INFER as a horizontal neighbor of ⟁CAUSE without leaving Yax:

(⟁CAUSE, Yax)  →horizontal→  (XCFE:INFER, Yax)     Δθ = 0

The operator has been reached but has not executed. XCFE must authorize the vertical step before execution occurs:

(XCFE:INFER, Yax)  →XCFE auth→  (XCFE:INFER, Sek)   Δθ = π/3
then Sek executes it.
Invariant. Phase membership is declared by the manifest; phase transition is authorized by XCFE. The graph therefore does not have to discover its ontology while running — the manifest has already compressed that ontology into Books; runtime unfolds only the pages it needs.

§45 — RCC — Field Graph Rendering Custom Content

The SVG 1.2 Working Draft defines a mechanism called RCC (Rendering Custom Content) — a generic framework that lets an SVG user agent recognise elements in a third-party namespace and transform them into SVG elements for rendering. The transformed elements are placed in a shadow DOM that does not pollute the source document. Critically, the transformation is bidirectional: interactive SVG mutations propagate back to the source elements. This makes RCC a more dynamic alternative to XSLT-based transformation.

RCC is architecturally identical to the relationship between field.xml and field.svg in the FieldDag system. field.xml is the authority document — structured like GraphML, with elements in the khanary:field namespace. field.svg is the static rendering we generate via FieldDag::to_svg(). Under RCC, a conforming SVG user agent would do that transformation live, reading field.xml and emitting the shadow SVG on the fly using prototype templates and event handlers declared in extensionDefs.

RCC architecture

An extensionDefs block declares how elements in a foreign namespace map to SVG prototypes and event handlers:

<extensionDefs namespace="http://graphml.graphdrawing.org/xmlns/1.0rc">
  <script type="text/ecmascript" xlink:href="graphml2svg.es"/>

  <elementDef name="graph">
    <prototype>
      <g><refContent/></g>
    </prototype>
    <script ev:event="SVGBindBegin">
      InitGraph(evt.target)
    </script>
  </elementDef>

  <elementDef name="node">
    <prototype>
      <g class="node">
        <rect width="100" height="100"/>
        <text/>
      </g>
    </prototype>
    <script ev:event="SVGBindBegin">
      var text = evt.target.shadowTree.getElementsByTagNameNS(SVG_NS, "text").item(0)
      var label = document.createTextNode(evt.target.getAttributeNS(null, "id"))
      text.appendChild(label)
    </script>
  </elementDef>

  <elementDef name="edge">
    <prototype><line class="edge"/></prototype>
    <script ev:event="SVGBindBegin">
      evt.target.parentNode.graph.addEdge(evt.target)
      evt.target.parentNode.graph.performLayout()
    </script>
  </elementDef>
</extensionDefs>

RCC concepts mapped to FieldDag

RCC conceptFieldDag equivalentWhere it lives
extensionDefsNode-kind render table in to_svg()field_dag.cpp
elementDef name="node"kind="track" → circle + labelto_svg() track branch
elementDef name="edge"FieldEdge → svg_edge_path()to_svg() edge loop
elementDef for tensorkind="tensor" → svg_tensor_arc()field_dag.cpp:svg_tensor_arc
<prototype>SVG element template per kind (circle/polygon/rect)Inlined in to_svg() string output
<refContent/>fold_map entries projected into arc verticessvg_tensor_arc()
SVGBindBegin eventRender-time attribute read (confidence, x/y/z, fold_map)to_svg() per-node dispatch
evt.target.shadowTreeZ-layer groups (0 substrate · 0.5 tensor arcs · 1 edges · 2 nodes)to_svg() <g id="layer-*">
Source document (field.xml)XML authority — topology + fold_map + kind attributesfield_dag.cpp:to_xml()
Shadow SVG (field.svg)Static render baked at startup / MCP callfield_dag.cpp:to_svg()

field.xml as the RCC source document

field.xml is the authority for topology and weight data. Its element vocabulary maps directly onto what RCC calls the "third-party namespace":

<field xmlns:kf="urn:khanary:field:v1">
  <kf:node id="ELIZA"   kind="track"   phase="Pop"  confidence="0.82" x="-0.5" y="0.87" z="0"/>
  <kf:node id="dds_sek" kind="tensor"  phase="Sek"/>
  <kf:edge from="Coordinator" to="ELIZA" type="delegate" weight="1.0"/>

  <kf:node id="dds_sek" kind="tensor" phase="Pop">
    <kf:fold_map>
      <kf:entry phase="Pop"   hook="on_read"  monitor="" action=""/>
      <kf:entry phase="Sek"   hook="on_write" monitor="gradient_norm > 10.0" action="warn_clip"/>
      <kf:entry phase="Ch'en" hook=""         monitor="confidence < 0.5"     action="promote_checkpoint"/>
      <kf:entry phase="Xul"   hook="on_gc"   monitor="" action=""/>
    </kf:fold_map>
  </kf:node>
</field>

A conforming RCC engine would process each kf:node via its elementDef prototype, cloning the appropriate SVG skeleton (circle for kind="track", polygon for kind="tensor", rect for kind="pipeline_stage") into the shadow tree, then firing SVGBindBegin to fill in position, color, and fold_map vertices from the source attributes.

RCC element definitions for the three node kinds

<extensionDefs namespace="urn:khanary:field:v1">

  <!-- track: micronaut at one dominant phase -->
  <elementDef name="node" kindFilter="track">
    <prototype>
      <g class="field-track">
        <circle r="22"/>
        <text class="track-label"/>
      </g>
    </prototype>
    <script ev:event="SVGBindBegin">
      var n = evt.target
      var g = n.shadowTree.querySelector("g")
      g.setAttribute("transform", "translate(" + svgX(n) + "," + svgY(n) + ")")
      g.querySelector("circle").setAttribute("fill", phaseColor(n.getAttribute("phase")))
      g.querySelector("text").textContent = n.getAttribute("id")
    </script>
  </elementDef>

  <!-- tensor: living tensor spanning multiple phases -->
  <elementDef name="node" kindFilter="tensor">
    <prototype>
      <g class="field-tensor">
        <polygon class="tensor-arc"/>   <!-- fold_map phase polygon -->
        <g class="tensor-hooks"/>       <!-- diamond markers per fold entry -->
        <text class="tensor-label"/>
      </g>
    </prototype>
    <script ev:event="SVGBindBegin">
      buildTensorArc(evt.target)        // reads fold_map children, projects onto C6 circle
    </script>
  </elementDef>

  <!-- pipeline_stage: C6 ordered processing stage -->
  <elementDef name="node" kindFilter="pipeline_stage">
    <prototype>
      <g class="field-pipeline">
        <rect width="60" height="28" rx="4"/>
        <text class="stage-label"/>
      </g>
    </prototype>
    <script ev:event="SVGBindBegin">
      bindPipelineStage(evt.target)
    </script>
  </elementDef>

  <!-- edge: delegate / horizontal_fold / vertical_fold arc -->
  <elementDef name="edge">
    <prototype><line class="field-edge"/></prototype>
    <script ev:event="SVGBindBegin">
      bindEdge(evt.target)   // sets x1/y1/x2/y2, stroke by type, dash by xcfe_required
    </script>
  </elementDef>

  <!-- fold_map: projects entry children into tensor arc vertices -->
  <elementDef name="fold_map">
    <prototype><refContent/></prototype>
  </elementDef>

</extensionDefs>

Static vs live rendering — two-mode architecture

ModeMechanismWhenOutput
Static (current)FieldDag::to_svg() — C++ bakes all SVG at call timeStartup write to data/world/field.svg; MCP field_dag_svg callSelf-contained SVG file; no runtime dependency
Live (RCC)SVG 1.2 RCC engine reads field.xml, applies extensionDefs, renders shadow SVGWhenever the user agent loads the SVG; reacts to XML mutationsDynamic; interactive mutations propagate back to field.xml

Both modes share the same authority document: field.xml. The static mode is the production path on systems without an RCC-capable SVG UA. The live mode is the design target — it gives PRIMEOS WebView2 the ability to render the semantic field graph interactively, with gravity-well edits and tensor arc updates reflecting immediately in the authority XML.

RCC and the shadow DOM parallel. RCC's shadow DOM — where rendered SVG elements live without polluting the source document — maps exactly onto the K'uhul product graph G_runtime = G_semantic □ G_phase. The source document (field.xml) is G_semantic: the authority topology. The shadow SVG is the rendered projection at a given execution phase θ. Mutations to the shadow via RCC are XCFE-gated updates back to G_semantic — the same authority-preserving mutation path as <learn> through Ch'en/Xul.

§46 — XSLT + SDM — Field Graph Layout Pipeline

The XSLT + SDM pipeline is a two-step offline transformation path that complements the live RCC mechanism (§45). Step 1 uses XSLT to translate the source graph XML into an SDM (Structured Drawing Model) intermediate. Step 2 feeds the SDM document to an SDM engine that applies a CSS-like stylesheet to produce the final SVG, delegating node/edge placement to an external graph layout library. Because layout is handled by a library rather than a hand-written algorithm, the pipeline correctly handles topologies that a naive XSLT tree walk cannot: cycles, nested (hierarchical) graphs, and mixed directed/undirected edges — all of which appear in the KHANARY field graph.

Step 1 — XSLT: field.xml → SDM intermediate

The XSLT strip-transforms the source graph, renaming attributes for the SDM engine. The sdm root wraps the output; node elements carry their @id; edge elements become link elements with from/to/isLink/directed:

<xsl:stylesheet version="1.0" xmlns:xsl="http://www.w3.org/1999/XSL/Transform">
  <xsl:output method="xml" version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" indent="yes"/>

  <xsl:template match="/">
    <sdm>
      <xsl:apply-templates select="node()"/>
    </sdm>
  </xsl:template>

  <xsl:template match="graph">
    <xsl:apply-templates select="node"/>
    <xsl:apply-templates select="edge"/>
  </xsl:template>

  <xsl:template match="node">
    <xsl:copy>
      <xsl:copy-of select="@id"/>
      <xsl:apply-templates select="graph"/>  <!-- nested sub-graphs -->
    </xsl:copy>
  </xsl:template>

  <xsl:template match="edge">
    <xsl:copy>
      <xsl:attribute name="isLink">true</xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:attribute name="from"><xsl:value-of select="@source"/></xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:attribute name="to"><xsl:value-of select="@target"/></xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:copy-of select="@directed"/>
    </xsl:copy>
  </xsl:template>
</xsl:stylesheet>

For field.xml this transform maps kf:node → node (preserving id, kind, phase, confidence) and kf:edge → link (with from/to/type/xcfe_required). Sub-micronaut composition in the Coordinator track becomes a nested <graph> child, which the recursive match="node" template handles automatically.

Step 2 — SDM stylesheet: layout + visual rules

The SDM stylesheet is CSS-like. The top-level rule selects the layout algorithm; subsequent rules declare visual representation per selector:

/* Layout algorithm — C6 radial for the field graph */
SDM {
  GraphLayout: Circular;   /* Circular positions nodes on a ring — natural for C6 */
}

/* Track nodes (micronaut, one dominant phase) */
node[kind='track'] {
  class: ilog.views.sdm.graphic.IlvGeneralNode;
  foreground: #22f2b3;     /* --asx-primary */
  fillColor1: #020617;     /* --asx-bg */
  label: @id;
  labelPosition: Center;
  shapeWidth: 44;
  shapeHeight: 44;
  shape: Ellipse;
}

/* Tensor nodes (living tensor, multi-phase fold_map) */
node[kind='tensor'] {
  class: ilog.views.sdm.graphic.IlvGeneralNode;
  foreground: #a855f7;     /* --asx-accent */
  fillColor1: #050b1c;
  label: @id;
  labelPosition: Center;
  shapeWidth: 48;
  shapeHeight: 48;
  shape: Diamond;
}

/* Pipeline stage nodes */
node[kind='pipeline_stage'] {
  class: ilog.views.sdm.graphic.IlvGeneralNode;
  foreground: #38bdf8;     /* --asx-secondary */
  fillColor1: #020b12;
  label: @id;
  shapeWidth: 70;
  shapeHeight: 28;
  shape: RoundedRect;
}

/* All links — directed by default */
link {
  class: ilog.views.IlvLinkImage;
  oriented: true;
  lineWidth: 1;
  foreground: rgba(148,163,184,0.5);
}

/* Delegate edges — bold */
link[type='delegate'] {
  lineWidth: 2;
  foreground: #22f2b3;
}

/* Horizontal fold — dashed, zero phase cost */
link[type='horizontal_fold'] {
  lineStyle: Dashed;
  foreground: #38bdf8;
}

/* Vertical fold — dotted, XCFE required */
link[type='vertical_fold'] {
  lineStyle: Dotted;
  foreground: #a855f7;
}

/* Undirected horizontal folds (Δθ = 0, bidirectional) */
link[directed='false'] {
  oriented: false;
}

Complex topology example — field graph patterns

The field graph contains all four topological features that the naive XSLT tree algorithm cannot handle. Each maps directly to a structural pattern in the semantic field:

<graph edgedefault="directed">
  <!-- Flat track nodes at C6 positions -->
  <node id="ELIZA"       kind="track" phase="Pop"/>
  <node id="Factory"     kind="track" phase="Wo"/>
  <node id="Coordinator" kind="track" phase="Yax"/>
  <node id="Coder"       kind="track" phase="Sek"/>
  <node id="ELIZA-1"     kind="track" phase="Ch'en"/>

  <!-- Nested graph: Coordinator sub-micronauts -->
  <node id="Coordinator">
    <graph>
      <node id="ELIZA"/>
      <node id="Factory"/>
      <node id="Librarian"/>
      <edge source="ELIZA"   target="Factory"/>
      <edge source="Factory" target="Librarian"/>
    </graph>
  </node>

  <!-- Delegation edges -->
  <edge source="Coordinator" target="ELIZA"   type="delegate"/>
  <edge source="Coordinator" target="Factory" type="delegate"/>

  <!-- Cycle: Coordinator delegates to ELIZA which feeds back -->
  <edge source="ELIZA"       target="Coordinator" type="delegate"/>

  <!-- Horizontal fold (bidirectional, Δθ = 0) -->
  <edge source="ELIZA" target="ELIZA-1" type="horizontal_fold" directed="false"/>

  <!-- Vertical fold (XCFE required, Δθ = π/3) -->
  <edge source="ELIZA" target="Factory" type="vertical_fold"/>
</graph>
Topology featureField graph instanceWhy naive tree walk fails
CycleCoordinator → ELIZA → Coordinator delegation loopDFS hits the cycle node a second time and either loops or silently drops it
Nested graphCoordinator contains sub-micronaut sub-graphRecursive match="node" handles nesting but loses parent-scope edge wiring
Undirected edgeHorizontal fold Δθ=0 — bidirectional semantic neighborsXSLT outputs a directed arrow; arrow direction is undefined for Δθ=0
Multi-edgeTwo edges between same pair with different type (delegate + horizontal_fold)SDM layout library routes parallel edges with offset curves; naive SVG draws them on top of each other

XSLT + SDM vs RCC — when to use each

CriterionXSLT + SDM (§46)RCC (§45)
Rendering timeOffline — baked SVG artifactLive — shadow SVG generated by UA on load
Layout algorithmExternal library (Circular, Tree, ForceDirected, …)Script in SVGBindBegin — must implement layout manually
Topology supportCycles, nested graphs, multi-edges handled by libraryDepends on script implementation
Bidirectional syncNo — SVG is a static snapshotYes — shadow mutations propagate back to source XML
PRIMEOS WebView2 useLoad field.svg directly; no SVG 1.2 UA requiredRequires SVG 1.2 RCC-capable UA
Authority documentfield.xml (read at transform time)field.xml (live reference; kept in sync)
Current stack statusFieldDag::to_svg() is the static equivalentDesign target for dynamic PRIMEOS rendering

The two mechanisms share the same authority document and the same visual vocabulary. The XSLT + SDM path is the production path today — to_svg() generates a self-contained SVG at startup and on every field_dag_svg MCP call. The RCC path is the design target: when a WebView2 host supports SVG 1.2, the static bake can be replaced by a live extensionDefs block that renders field.xml directly and propagates interactive edits back to the semantic field graph.

§47 — Relational Micronauts — Graph as Execution Plan

Every XSLT/SDM graph structure has a direct K'UHUL Relational Micronaut reading: every <node> is a micronaut instance, every <edge> is a relational verb call, every nested <graph> is a sub-micronaut cluster. The XSLT transform maps graph topology to the SDM intermediate; the SDM stylesheet gives it visual form; the K'UHUL execution layer gives it behavioral semantics. The result: a graph structure that simultaneously describes a visual layout, a micronaut roster, and a live execution plan.

Architecture merge map

XSLT/SDM LAYER                    K'UHUL RELATIONAL LAYER
────────────────                  ──────────────────────

<graph>                    →      Relational Network
<node>                     →      Micronaut Instance
<edge>                     →      Relational Verb (@call, @delegate)
edgedefault="directed"     →      @direction (bidirectional/uni)
directed="false"           →      @oriented: false
nested <graph>             →      Sub-micronaut cluster
GraphLayout: Tree          →      Hierarchical micronaut tree
node { class: ... }        →      @identity, @category
link { oriented: true }    →      @relation (directed)
link[directed='false']     →      @relation (undirected, Δθ=0)
label: @id                 →      @name, @id

1 · The merged XSLT — K'UHUL_RELATIONAL.xsl

The XSLT transforms <node> elements to micronaut instances (preserving @id and @category) and <edge> elements to relational verb links. The @verb attribute is derived from the edge's directed flag: directed edges default to @call; undirected edges become @relate (horizontal fold, Δθ=0).

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<xsl:stylesheet version="1.0"
                xmlns:xsl="http://www.w3.org/1999/XSL/Transform"
                xmlns:k="https://kuhul.io/relational">

  <xsl:output method="xml" version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" indent="yes"/>

  <xsl:template match="/">
    <sdm xmlns:k="https://kuhul.io/relational">
      <xsl:apply-templates select="node()"/>
    </sdm>
  </xsl:template>

  <xsl:template match="graph">
    <xsl:apply-templates select="node"/>
    <xsl:apply-templates select="edge"/>
  </xsl:template>

  <xsl:template match="node">
    <xsl:copy>
      <xsl:attribute name="id"><xsl:value-of select="@id"/></xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:attribute name="type">micronaut</xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:attribute name="category"><xsl:value-of select="@category"/></xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:if test="graph">
        <xsl:apply-templates select="graph"/>  <!-- nested sub-cluster -->
      </xsl:if>
    </xsl:copy>
  </xsl:template>

  <xsl:template match="edge">
    <xsl:copy>
      <xsl:attribute name="isLink">true</xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:attribute name="from"><xsl:value-of select="@source"/></xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:attribute name="to"><xsl:value-of select="@target"/></xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:attribute name="verb">
        <xsl:choose>
          <xsl:when test="@directed='false'">@relate</xsl:when>
          <xsl:otherwise>@call</xsl:otherwise>
        </xsl:choose>
      </xsl:attribute>
      <xsl:copy-of select="@directed"/>
      <xsl:copy-of select="@verb"/>  <!-- preserve explicit verb if declared -->
    </xsl:copy>
  </xsl:template>

</xsl:stylesheet>

2 · The merged SDM stylesheet — K'UHUL_RELATIONAL.css

Category-based node styles; verb-based link styles. The node:has(graph) selector promotes nodes with nested sub-graphs to cluster nodes with a visible container boundary. In production, replace hardcoded hex values with ASX CSS variables (--asx-primary, --asx-secondary, etc.).

SDM {
  GraphLayout: Tree;
  nodeSpacing: 50;
  levelSpacing: 100;
}

node {
  class: kuhul.views.graphic.MicronautNode;
  foreground: black;
  fillColor1: #4a90e2;
  label: @id;
  labelPosition: Center;
  shapeWidth: 120;
  shapeHeight: 60;
  borderColor: #1a3a6e;
  borderWidth: 2;
  cornerRadius: 8;
}

node[category='executive'] { fillColor1: #e74c3c; shapeWidth: 140; shapeHeight: 70; }
node[category='cognitive']  { fillColor1: #3498db; }
node[category='storage']    { fillColor1: #2ecc71; }
node[category='creative']   { fillColor1: #f39c12; }

node:has(graph) {
  class: kuhul.views.ClusterNode;
  fillColor1: #9b59b6;
  shapeWidth: 200; shapeHeight: 150;
  clusterBackground: #ecf0f1;
}

link {
  class: kuhul.views.RelationalLink;
  oriented: true;
  strokeWidth: 2;
  arrowSize: 10;
}

link[directed='false'] { oriented: false; strokeDash: dashed; strokeColor: #666; }

link[verb='@call']     { strokeColor: #e74c3c; }
link[verb='@delegate'] { strokeColor: #3498db; }
link[verb='@compose']  { strokeColor: #2ecc71; }
link[verb='@eval']     { strokeColor: #f39c12; }
link[verb='@relate']   { strokeColor: #95a5a6; }

link::label {
  visible: true; label: @verb; labelPosition: Center;
  fontSize: 10; background: white; padding: 2; cornerRadius: 4;
}

3 · Graph input — kuhul_relational_graph.xml

The input graph carries @category on nodes and @verb on edges. Nested <graph> inside ORCH-µ declares its sub-micronaut cluster. The MEM-µ ↔ FILE-µ edge appears twice: once directed (@call) and once undirected (@relate), representing both the imperative call and the bidirectional semantic neighbor relationship.

<graph edgedefault="directed" xmlns:k="https://kuhul.io/relational">

  <node id="ORCH-µ" category="executive">
    <graph>
      <node id="REASON-µ" category="cognitive"/>
      <node id="CREATE-µ" category="creative"/>
      <node id="PLAN-µ"   category="executive"/>
      <edge source="REASON-µ" target="PLAN-µ"   verb="@call"/>
      <edge source="CREATE-µ" target="REASON-µ" verb="@eval"/>
      <edge source="PLAN-µ"   target="REASON-µ" verb="@call"/>
    </graph>
  </node>

  <node id="REASON-µ"   category="cognitive"/>
  <node id="MEM-µ"      category="storage"/>
  <node id="CODE-µ"     category="cognitive"/>
  <node id="GRAPHICS-µ" category="creative"/>
  <node id="FILE-µ"     category="storage"/>
  <node id="NET-µ"      category="cognitive"/>
  <node id="INFER-µ"    category="cognitive"/>

  <edge source="ORCH-µ"    target="REASON-µ"   verb="@delegate"/>
  <edge source="ORCH-µ"    target="CREATE-µ"   verb="@delegate"/>
  <edge source="ORCH-µ"    target="PLAN-µ"     verb="@delegate"/>
  <edge source="REASON-µ"  target="MEM-µ"      verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="REASON-µ"  target="CODE-µ"     verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="REASON-µ"  target="INFER-µ"    verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="CREATE-µ"  target="GRAPHICS-µ" verb="@compose"/>
  <edge source="CREATE-µ"  target="CODE-µ"     verb="@compose"/>
  <edge source="PLAN-µ"    target="MEM-µ"      verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="PLAN-µ"    target="REASON-µ"   verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="MEM-µ"     target="FILE-µ"     verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="CODE-µ"    target="REASON-µ"   verb="@eval"/>
  <edge source="INFER-µ"   target="REASON-µ"   verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="INFER-µ"   target="MEM-µ"      verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="NET-µ"     target="CODE-µ"     verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="NET-µ"     target="FILE-µ"     verb="@call"/>
  <edge source="GRAPHICS-µ" target="REASON-µ"  verb="@eval"/>

  <!-- Undirected: MEM-µ ↔ FILE-µ semantic neighbor (Δθ = 0) -->
  <edge source="MEM-µ" target="FILE-µ" directed="false" verb="@relate"/>

</graph>

5 · K'UHUL Relational Micronaut definitions

The (⟁RELATIONAL⟁) sigil declares a micronaut whose primary interface is its @knows relation map and its @verb methods. Relational verbs carry K'uhul phase semantics: @delegate fires at Wo (plan), @call at Sek (execute), @eval at Ch'en (verify), @relate at Pop (horizontal fold, Δθ=0, no phase transition required), @aggregate at Xul (commit).

(⟁RELATIONAL⟁) ORCH-µ {
  @identity: { @name: "Orchestrator", @role: "coordinator", @category: "executive" }

  @knows: {
    @agents: ["REASON-µ", "CREATE-µ", "PLAN-µ"],
    @relations: {
      "REASON-µ": { @verb: "@delegate", @directed: true },
      "CREATE-µ": { @verb: "@delegate", @directed: true },
      "PLAN-µ":   { @verb: "@delegate", @directed: true }
    }
  }

  @think: (goal) => {
    reasoning = @call("REASON-µ", "@think", { problem: goal })    // Wo→Sek
    creation  = @call("CREATE-µ", "@think", { seed:    goal })
    planning  = @call("PLAN-µ",   "@think", { goal:    goal })
    return @aggregate([reasoning, creation, planning])             // Xul commit
  }
}

(⟁RELATIONAL⟁) REASON-µ {
  @identity: { @name: "Reasoner", @role: "logic", @category: "cognitive" }

  @knows: {
    @agents: ["MEM-µ", "CODE-µ", "INFER-µ"],
    @relations: {
      "MEM-µ":   { @verb: "@call", @directed: true },
      "CODE-µ":  { @verb: "@call", @directed: true },
      "INFER-µ": { @verb: "@call", @directed: true }
    }
  }

  @think: (problem) => {
    context      = @call("MEM-µ",   "@recall",  { query:       problem })
    computation  = @call("CODE-µ",  "@compute", { problem:     problem })
    alternatives = @call("INFER-µ", "@infer",   { observation: problem })
    return @aggregate([context, computation, alternatives])
  }
}

(⟁RELATIONAL⟁) MEM-µ {
  @identity: { @name: "Memory", @role: "persistence", @category: "storage" }

  @knows: {
    @agents: ["FILE-µ"],
    @relations: {
      "FILE-µ": [
        { @verb: "@call",   @directed: true  },   // Sek: imperative write/read
        { @verb: "@relate", @directed: false }    // Pop: bidirectional neighbor (Δθ=0)
      ]
    }
  }

  @remember: (fact)  => @call("FILE-µ", "@write", { content: fact })
  @recall:   (query) => @call("FILE-µ", "@read",  { query:   query })
}

(⟁RELATIONAL⟁) CREATE-µ {
  @identity: { @name: "Creator", @role: "generation", @category: "creative" }
  @knows: { @agents: ["GRAPHICS-µ", "CODE-µ"],
    @relations: {
      "GRAPHICS-µ": { @verb: "@compose", @directed: true },
      "CODE-µ":     { @verb: "@compose", @directed: true }
    }
  }
  @think: (seed) => {
    visual = @call("GRAPHICS-µ", "@render",   { seed: seed })
    code   = @call("CODE-µ",     "@generate", { seed: seed })
    score  = @call("REASON-µ",   "@eval",     { output: [visual, code] })   // Ch'en
    return { visual, code, score }
  }
}

/* INFER-µ, CODE-µ, GRAPHICS-µ, NET-µ, FILE-µ — leaf/near-leaf micronauts */
(⟁RELATIONAL⟁) INFER-µ {
  @identity: { @name: "Inference", @role: "inference", @category: "cognitive" }
  @knows: { @agents: ["REASON-µ", "MEM-µ"],
    @relations: {
      "REASON-µ": { @verb: "@call", @directed: true },
      "MEM-µ":    { @verb: "@call", @directed: true }
    }
  }
  @infer: (obs) => @call("REASON-µ", "@think", { problem: obs })
}

6 · Relational verb → K'uhul phase mapping

VerbDirectionK'uhul phaseΔθXCFE gateSDM link color
@delegatedirectedWo — plan/dispatchπ/3sandbox level check#3498db (blue)
@calldirectedSek — executeπ/3capability + sandbox#e74c3c (red)
@composedirectedYax — coordinate0 or π/3capability check#2ecc71 (green)
@evaldirectedCh'en — verifyπ/3evidence threshold#f39c12 (orange)
@relateundirectedPop — horizontal fold0none — free traversal#95a5a6 (gray dashed)
@aggregatedirectedXul — commitπ/3Ch'en approval—

@relate is the only verb with Δθ=0 — it is a horizontal fold, not a phase transition. MEM-µ and FILE-µ are semantic neighbors at the same phase level; no XCFE authorization is required to traverse the edge. directed="false" in the graph XML is the structural declaration of a horizontal fold.

7 · Execution pipeline

1. GRAPH INPUT (kuhul_relational_graph.xml)
   <graph edgedefault="directed">
     <node id="ORCH-µ" category="executive">...</node>
     <edge source="ORCH-µ" target="REASON-µ" verb="@delegate"/>
   </graph>

2. XSLT TRANSFORM (K'UHUL_RELATIONAL.xsl)
   <edge source="…" target="…" verb="@delegate"/>
   → <edge from="ORCH-µ" to="REASON-µ" verb="@delegate" isLink="true"/>

3. SDM STYLESHEET (K'UHUL_RELATIONAL.css)
   node[category='executive'] { fillColor1: #e74c3c; }
   link[verb='@delegate']     { strokeColor: #3498db; }

4. RELATIONAL MICRONAUT EXECUTION
   ORCH-µ.@think(goal)
     → @call("REASON-µ", "@think", {problem: goal})     // Wo→Sek
     → @call("CREATE-µ", "@think", {seed:    goal})
     → @call("PLAN-µ",   "@think", {goal:    goal})
     → @aggregate([reasoning, creation, planning])       // Xul

5. LEARNING LOOP
   Every @call  → @learn feedback (Ch'en phase)
   Every @eval  → confidence score update
   Every @relate → horizontal fold gram read/write

6. SVG VISUALIZATION OUTPUT
   Micronauts as category-colored nodes
   Verbs as colored, labeled directed/undirected links
   Layout: Tree (hierarchical ORCH-µ root) or Circular (C6 field)

8 · Six-layer architecture

LAYER 6: VISUALIZATION
│  XSLT → SDM → SVG
│  • Graph layout (Tree / Circular / ForceDirected)
│  • Node styling (category-based)
│  • Edge styling (verb-based, directed/undirected)

LAYER 5: RELATIONAL TOPOLOGY
│  Graph structure
│  • Nodes   = Micronauts (identity + category)
│  • Edges   = Relational Verbs (directed or undirected)
│  • Nested graphs = Sub-micronaut clusters

LAYER 4: RELATIONAL VERBS
│  @think · @learn · @eval · @call · @delegate
│  @compose · @aggregate · @relate

LAYER 3: MICRONAUT EXECUTION
│  ORCH-µ · REASON-µ · CREATE-µ · PLAN-µ
│  MEM-µ  · CODE-µ   · GRAPHICS-µ · FILE-µ
│  NET-µ  · INFER-µ

LAYER 2: K'UHUL PHYSICS
│  Pop(φ) · Wo(F) · Yax(G) · Sek(γ) · Ch'en(θ) · Xul(Π)
│  Arc weights: effective[i→j] = arc_w[i][j] × cos(angles[j] − angles[i])

LAYER 1: HARDWARE
│  CPU · GPU (DirectML / D3D11) · Network

Complete mapping table

XSLT/SDM conceptK'UHUL Relational concept
<graph>Relational Network — full micronaut roster + verb topology
<node>Micronaut Instance (@identity, @category, @knows)
<edge>Relational Verb Call — one of @call/@delegate/@compose/@eval/@relate/@aggregate
edgedefault="directed"@direction: true — Sek/Wo/Ch'en phase transitions required
directed="false"@direction: false — @relate — horizontal fold, Δθ=0, no XCFE gate
nested <graph>Sub-micronaut cluster — coordinator.@roster inner graph
GraphLayout: TreeHierarchical micronaut tree (Coordinator root → delegated branches)
node { class: ... }@identity, @category, @capabilities
link { oriented: true }@relation directed — carries phase and XCFE gate
link[directed='false']@relation undirected — horizontal fold, O(1) manifest neighbor
label: @id@name, @id — manifest key for O(1) roster lookup
@sourcefrom — calling micronaut
@targetto — receiving micronaut
SDM engine layout passGravity field score — dispatch_blend() top-k weight vector
Every graph is an execution plan. The XSLT/SDM pipeline and the K'UHUL Relational layer are two readings of the same artifact. The graph is the topology; the XSLT is the schema translation; the SDM stylesheet is the visual contract; the relational micronaut definitions are the behavioral contract. Every edge is a verb. Every node is an addressable manifest key. Every nested graph is a sub-micronaut cluster with its own inner topology — recursively composable to arbitrary depth, following the same manifest expansion rule as "⟁COORDINATOR": ["⟁ELIZA", "⟁CODER", "routing", "⟁Ch'en"].
